---
name: no-sophistry-in-lesson-copy
description: "Lesson copy must be plain, natural and personable; define the idea first (contrapositive = logical equivalent), no \"promise / depends on / nothing to stand on\" talk; log every new correction."
metadata:
  node_type: memory
  type: feedback
  originSessionId: 6f1c3429-949a-4b8a-b0c8-a960c83f510f
  modified: 2026-10-12T02:07:52.687Z
---

When writing or editing LSAT lesson copy, say what the thing is in plain words first, then show it. Cut sophistry: no "a rule makes a promise", no "the left side depends on the right", no "nothing to stand on", no vague "the other one". Keep it natural, intuitive, personable, in his teaching voice.

**Why:** On 2026-10-11 Joshua rejected the first contrapositive lesson as "terribly done", then rejected the rewrite for "a lot of sophistry". He wants the contrapositive taught as the logical equivalent: the same rule said a different way, interchangeable, equally valid. His own session transcripts say exactly that.

**How to apply:**
- Read `wiki/contrapositive-teaching.md` in the vault before any contrapositive work. It holds his own wording and the mistakes he warns about.
- Order for a concept lesson: what it is, see it work, how to do it, what it is not, the steps in short, why it matters.
- Every time he corrects wording, add it to `wiki/COPY-CORRECTIONS.md` the same turn, with the reason. Read that file before writing new notes. This is how the same mistake is not repeated.
- Title lessons by topic ("Contrapositive"). Card fronts ask a specific question and the back gives the answer first. "Correct", not "Right".
- Related: [[no-em-dashes-no-filler-in-joshuas-writing]], [[lsat-teaching-voice-standard]], [[no-internal-labels-in-student-lessons]].
- Never say the contrapositive is "the one other thing" a rule tells you (his 2026-10-11 correction). Say equivalent and interchangeable: know one, you know the other.
- Do not use the raffle/bicycle as the example in new contrapositive or arrow material (the contrapositive page is raffle-free as of 2026-10-12; earlier reference steps, opening lessons and foundations source still use it). Use a fresh everyday example (current contrapositive note: elevator moving, doors closed).
- Contrapositive lesson: no thought experiment or guess card (it confused him). Follow the source HTML explanation directly in close language, definition first, equivalence stated, "left side / right side of the arrow". Never mention Logic Games. Never reuse the source coffee or breathing/air examples.
- Rules say "the power MUST be on". Avoid the word "foreign". Voice = his Intro to LR bootcamp: plain claim, short stacked landings, "Here's the thing", dry wit. A cheeky exclamation on the one key word is OK in the contrapositive lesson, with a one-time punch-in and glow; motion must carry the reasoning and respect reduced motion.
- NEVER show negation as a squiggle (~) beside an idea, anywhere, including answer choices and the reference practice. Use the word "not" or a diagonal strike from the idea's top-left to bottom-right corner (component Neg / Notation in ContrapositiveVisuals.tsx; the ~ is gone from the conditionals module as of 2026-10-12). Write the contrapositive as a plain sentence early.
- Anything that is wrong must LOOK wrong: red card, X badge, the word "Wrong" (reverse and inverse cards). Look-alike wrong answers and the right one never share a table. No repeated content; high-end, sleek visuals for anything interactive.
---

description: The finished Contrapositive lesson page (2026-10-12) is Joshua's quality bar for every concept lecture; the full do/don't list and build process are saved in bootcamp-builder's teaching-preferences.
metadata:

---

Joshua called the finished Contrapositive page "perfect" and wants every other concept lecture built to the same standard, reworked (not copied) for each topic.

**Why:** he spent a long session correcting it; the accepted result encodes his taste (short, if-then sentences, bold steps, one example plus one fresh example, sleek varied effects, specific concise feedback, narrated video).

**How to apply:** before building or revising any lecture or note, read the "Gold standard" section of `.claude/skills/bootcamp-builder/references/teaching-preferences.md` and `wiki/COPY-CORRECTIONS.md`. Run the humanizer skill on the prose. See [[no-sophistry-in-lesson-copy]] and [[every-drill-gets-its-own-walkthrough]].

As of 2026-10-11 the Conditional bootcamp (opening course, drills) has not been released to students. Joshua: "this is not published for students yet, so you can take your time."

**Why:** reordering drills or questions, renaming step ids, or changing saved-state shapes cannot strand real student work yet.
**How to apply:** prefer the cleanest design over backward-compatibility shims for conditional drill state. Check again before relying on this later: once it ships, [[student-work-must-be-preserved]] applies in full.

# Bootcamp teaching preferences

Captured 2026-10-11 from Joshua's conditional-bootcamp conversation. These are requirements for future builds where applicable, not proof that any implementation has passed acceptance.

## General standards confirmed by repeated corrections

- Compare the topic against internal corpus sources before drafting. Preserve source provenance and distinguish a source claim from Joshua's decision. Ingest new clips through the ingest skill before relying on them. Never infer a video transcript from its title.
- Explain the concept in plain language, then develop it through a concrete familiar example. Introduce notation after meaning. A short definition alone is not a lesson. Avoid recursive definitions and tongue twisters.
- Break explanations into readable sections with clear visual hierarchy, larger text, generous spacing, and short paragraphs. Inspect the actual rendered page, including the docked browser width and mobile, rather than treating JSX inspection as visual approval.
- Bring meaningful interaction early, after enough teaching to attempt it. Feedback explains the reasoning and distinguishes given facts from conclusions of a rule. Do not merely cycle reveal buttons and call that practice.
- Use color, emphasis, and motion to direct attention to the entire relevant idea. Preserve complete conditions, restrictions, timing, modality, and the difference between a rule and a fact.
- Preserve existing teaching media while revising. Check that videos still exist and play in their intended lesson. New media must earn its place in the teaching flow.
...

28	## Necessary-condition lesson: explicitly approved wording
29	
30	Joshua supplied and approved this copy on 2026-10-11:
31	
32	> **A necessary condition is a requirement. It has to be met, but meeting it might not be enough.**
33	> For example, you have to be a student to be enrolled in law school. That makes being a student **necessary for law-school enrollment**.
34	> But being a student doesn’t mean you’re enrolled in law school. You could be studying history instead.
35	> **Being a student is required. It doesn’t establish enrollment on its own.**
36	
37	Keep “might not”: a condition may be both necessary and sufficient. Do not replace this with “something another condition can’t be true without.”
38	
39	Explicit page corrections:
40	- Emphasize the complete consequent, “then you are a student,” and identify “you are a student” as the condition. Do not teach isolated “then” as a standalone necessary-condition indicator.
41	- Law student / history student / not a student should be vertical case tabs. Keep keyboard navigation, visible selected state, and mobile stacking. This is a decision for this case comparison, not a mandate for every interaction.
42	- Remove “Replay emphasis”; use a gentle continuous pulse for this page, with a static reduced-motion equivalent. This does not authorize constant pulsing on every page.
43	- Make clear which facts are given and which follow. If a history case says not enrolled, make it explicitly given rather than inferring it from studying history.
44	
45	Content preservation checks for the conditional sequence:
46	- Necessary examples retain only, only if, only when, only where, always, requires, must; explain their meaning in the complete sentence and preserve modality/scope. This is the existing lesson coverage, not a context-free replacement dictionary.
47	- Sufficient Group 1 stays on-page: if; when/whenever; any/anytime; all; every/every time; in order to; people who; each.
48	- Keep the reference-letter qualifiers, including current and accepted for review. A distractor should reveal a reasoning error, not reward guessing.

50	## Must arrow: correction and open work
51	
52	- Confirmed title: **Must arrow**, not “Read the must arrow.”
53	- Teach the direction explicitly with a familiar relationship: in this convention sufficient on the left, necessary on the right, arrow pointing right. The left condition guarantees the right **if the left condition is true**. The arrow alone asserts neither condition as a fact, neither causation nor chronological order, and no reverse inference.
54	- The lead “A must arrow shows what has to be true if another condition is true” was rejected as too abstract on 2026-10-11. Do not preserve it as approved wording. Joshua wants the direction shown and explained directly.
55	- Current provisional implementation: “The must arrow points from left to right. If the left side is true, the right side must be true.” The new LSAT Lab clip (jeDnykOrswY) supports certainty at 0:31 and left requiring right at 8:19, per the source review in this session. This wording is not yet user-approved.

199	## Gold standard: the Contrapositive lesson page (approved 2026-10-12)
200	
201	Joshua called the finished page "perfect". Treat it as the template for every other concept lecture: same order, same density, same rules. Do not copy its wording or its examples. Rework the structure for the new topic and pick fresh examples.
202	
203	**Where it lives:** `ai-os-projects/lsatu-conditional-bootcamp/src/bootcamps/conditionals/components/` (`OpeningCourseContent.tsx` `ContrapositiveNote` and `FourRules`, `ContrapositiveVisuals.tsx`, `contrapositive-visuals.css`), commit `383747e9` plus the uncommitted follow-ups of 2026-10-12. Video project: `ai-os-projects/contrapositive-video/lecture` (`script.json`, `index.html`, `build_audio.py`). Full correction log: `wiki/COPY-CORRECTIONS.md`.
204	
205	### Page shape (about 600 words of text, seven sections)
206	
207	1. **What it is.** Plain definition first, in a navy panel, then one short example. No run-up.
208	2. **Why it matters or what it gives you.** Two or three sentences. The key term pulses, one exclamation at most.
209	3. **Watch the lesson.** A 60 to 90 second narrated video placed after the idea is introduced. Captions track, poster image, written fallback that only renders when opened or when the video fails.
210	4. **How to write it.** Two bold steps (Step 1 and Step 2), then an interactive demo with "Step 1. Click here to ..." and "Step 2. Click here to ..." buttons. A second demo uses a fresh, simple, intuitive example where one side already starts negated, so the rule that two nots cancel is shown, not stated. Each demo shows the rule as an if-then sentence above the diagram and the result as an if-then sentence below.
211	5. **What it is not.** One line, then slim cards: red "Wrong" for each look-alike, green "Right" for the answer. Badge, one-line change, diagram. Nothing else.
212	6. **A short habit paragraph.** One paragraph, no list.
213	7. **Pop Quiz.** Centered title with a letter-pop and light-sweep effect. Four items, one at a time. Ideas shown as short phrases, never letters. Right answer explanation is one sentence (the answer as a natural if-then). Wrong answer explanation is "Not that one." plus one specific reason about that choice. No intro paragraph, no category tag lines.
214	
215	### What Joshua wanted (keep)
216	
217	- One main example carried through the page, plus one fresh second example. Never the same example in more places than that, and never an example already used on another page.
218	- Natural if-then sentences for every rule and result. Diagrams support the sentence; they never replace it.
219	- Bold numbered steps. Controls say what to click. The student drives; nothing autoplays.
220	- Colour carries meaning and travels with the idea (amber sufficient, teal necessary). Wrong looks wrong (red, X); right looks right (green, check).
221	- Sleek, high-end visuals. A new effect for each new moment; never reuse the same trick. Every animation has a still equivalent and stops under reduced motion.
222	- Short. When a page feels like too much, cut sections, not words: remove whole parts that repeat what the video, a demo or the quiz already does.
223	- Ideas in quiz diagrams are condensed statements ("On the list", "Wristband"), not letter codes.
224	- Narrated video in his voice standard, AI voice acceptable, built from the same wording as the page.
225	
226	### What Joshua rejected (never repeat)
227	
228	- A squiggle (~) for negation anywhere. Use the word "not" or a diagonal strike from the idea's top left to bottom right.
229	- The raffle, coat, coffee, breathing, elevator, door and any example already used elsewhere. "Door", "patterns" and "move" as student words.
230	- Sophistry and obligation metaphors ("promise", "depends on", "nothing to stand on"). Stock encouragement and run-ups ("Here's the good news", "Every time:").
231	- Content repeated on the page, a legend that restates what a pill already says, a paragraph before the quiz explaining the quiz, tag lines that add nothing.
232	- Complicated interactives: the first light-bulb stage (wires, chips, extra buttons) and the "four worlds" picker were both cut as too busy. One idea, one control, one sentence.
233	- Long answer explanations. Explanations that talk about the diagram ("both ideas get a not") instead of saying the answer in plain English.
234	- Letters standing in for ideas.
235	
236	### Process that produced it (work backwards from this)
237	
238	1. Write the section list above for the new topic before any copy. Cut anything the video, a demo or the quiz already covers.
239	2. Draft prose in Joshua's voice, then run the `humanizer` skill over it and the corrections log. No em dashes.
240	3. Build the visuals one at a time, check each on the live preview (not only in tests), and ask what is new about each effect.

wiki/COPY-CORRECTIONS.md:77:Joshua marked four parts of the V6 opening: "Read each whole idea," "Find what the statements guarantee," the abstract first sentence about information, and the paragraph combining application and linking. They named goals without showing a useful action or explanation. Replace them with numbered, separate scenarios. Show the fact and rule that prove a conclusion about one case; separately show two matching rules and their new conditional conclusion. Labels should identify actual inputs or outputs. Review the whole note for repeated umbrella instructions and make task instructions executable. This follows the teaching voice: specific, concrete, short sentences, with air between distinct ideas.
wiki/COPY-CORRECTIONS.md:81:Joshua rejected definitions that remained abstract and an explanation that did not clearly distinguish applying a rule from linking rules. Define the inputs with examples, then show the matching step and exact output. A case fact plus a rule proves something about that case. Two connecting rules produce another conditional rule without establishing a case exists. Adding a fact to the chain is a separate step. Explain all four fact situations and a hidden chain revealed by contraposition rather than leaving them implicit in an interactive model. Rules include general logical relationships, not only policies or obligations.

# Grill Me

One skill, self-contained. It holds both the **interview method** (Step 2) and the **capture discipline** (Steps 1 and 3): every answer lands in a durable markdown file, so nothing is lost as context fills up. It does not call the vendor `grilling` skill: the method is written inline below so this skill can add the capture steps around it and keep working even if `grilling` changes upstream.

If the topic is implementation work, finish the interview here, then hand the agreed design to `claudex-loop` to plan and build.

## Step 1: set up the capture file (BEFORE the first question)

1. Read the applicable `AGENTS.md` and `CLAUDE.md`, then only the context pages and prior captures relevant to the topic. Starter-kit context lives in `context/`; in an established project follow its declared routes instead of creating a duplicate knowledge store. Reuse facts already supplied; ask about gaps, changes, decisions, and tradeoffs. Installing the skill does not start an interview.
2. Create the capture file at `brainstorms/{YYYY-MM-DD}-{topic-slug}.md` (create `brainstorms/` if missing). Every capture lives here, one predictable home regardless of topic. Do NOT scatter captures into project folders. A polished deliverable (plan, map, spec) may later move into the relevant `projects/` folder; the raw capture always stays in `brainstorms/`.
   - Get today's real date from the clock or shell (`Get-Date -Format yyyy-MM-dd` in PowerShell, `date +%F` in Bash).
   - Never overwrite an existing capture. Same date and topic: add a time or unique suffix and create exclusively. Resume an existing capture only when the user asks or clearly refers to that session; read it first and continue its question numbering.
   - No topic supplied or inferable: create an `untitled-discovery` capture with goal pending, ask what to explore, record the chosen topic and goal in that same file.
3. Write the header immediately: title, date, goal of the session, empty "Open flags" section.
4. Tell the user where you are saving, in one line.

## Step 2: run the interview

Interview the user relentlessly until you reach a shared understanding. Map this as a **design tree**: every decision branches into the decisions that hang off it.

Work the tree in **rounds**. The **frontier** is every decision whose prerequisites are already settled: the questions you can ask _now_ without guessing at answers you have not heard yet. Ask the whole frontier in one round: number each question and give your recommended answer. Then wait for the user's answers before the next round.

Format a round like so:

```
**Q1 - <question title>**: <question body, may be multiple paragraphs, including choices>

Recommended: <your recommended answer>

---

**Q2 - <question title>**: ...

Recommended: ...
```

Each round the user answers reshapes the tree: settled decisions push the frontier outward and unblock questions that depended on them. Recompute the frontier and ask the next round. A question whose answer depends on another question still open in this round belongs to a _later_ round, not this one.

Finding _facts_ is your job, never the user's. When a frontier question needs a fact from the environment (filesystem, tools, live site), look it up or dispatch a sub-agent; never ask the user for anything you could find yourself. Do not block on it: a running exploration is an unsettled prerequisite, so only the questions downstream of it wait; ask the rest of the frontier now. The _decisions_ are the user's: put each to them and wait.

The session is done when the frontier is empty: every branch visited, nothing silently assumed. Do not act on it until the user confirms you have reached a shared understanding.

Two adjustments the capture layer imposes on the method:

- For **decisions**, give a recommended answer grounded in context and label it as a recommendation. For **personal or business facts**, ask neutrally. Never lead with invented facts, and never save your inference as the user's answer.
- When the user **cannot answer** something, capture it as a flag with the right owner and move on. Do not stall the round on it.

## Step 3: the checkpoint rule (non-negotiable)

After EVERY round of answers, BEFORE you ask the next round:

- Append one structured entry per answered question: the question, the key facts and decisions from the answer (in the user's words where wording matters), and any flags (things they could not answer, plus who should).
- Update the running summary when a later answer changes it. Preserve the original entry, mark it superseded, and link to the correcting answer so the reasoning stays traceable.
- Read back the saved entries before continuing. If the write fails, say so, keep the answers visible in chat, and do not claim they were saved or collect more answers without a working checkpoint.
- Only then ask the next round.

Never let two rounds pile up in your head before writing. The file, not your context, is the source of truth. Never make the user ask you to save progress.

Keep user-confirmed facts, tentative ideas, your recommendations, and unresolved questions distinguishable in the capture.

## Capture file structure

```
# {Topic}: Brainstorm / Discovery Notes
Date: {date} · Goal: {one line}
Status: in progress / paused / complete
Context sources: {relevant existing pages, if any}

## Summary / key decisions
(running synthesis, updated as you go)

## Q&A log
### Round 1
#### Q1 - {topic}
- Asked: {question}
- Recommended: {your recommendation, labeled}
- Captured: {user-confirmed facts and decisions, in their words where it matters}
- Tentative: {unconfirmed ideas, clearly labeled}
- Flags: {open item -> owner}
#### Q2 - ...
### Round 2
...

## Open flags (pending input)
- {item} -> {who can answer}
```

## At the end or on pause

Respect an explicit stop immediately; do not add a new question. Near the end, offer a completeness backstop ("anything we haven't touched?"). Then:

- Read the capture for contradictions or gaps. Mark unresolved conflicts explicitly; do not choose a business fact on the user's behalf. Update status and resume point.
- For sessions explicitly about building or updating AI OS context, merge user-confirmed durable facts and preferences into the appropriate existing context page, with a dated link back to the capture. Preserve unrelated content. Record confirmed meaningful decisions in the existing decision log without duplicating entries. Unknowns, brainstorm ideas, and your recommendations stay labeled in the capture until confirmed.
- For a plan or design interview without a request to update canonical context, the raw capture is the output. Suggest `/link` for routing a useful result, or `/level-up` for turning a selected opportunity into one improvement. Do not run either automatically.
- Ensure the operating manual or an existing index routes to `brainstorms/` when capture routing is absent, using the project's conventions and preserving shared manual parity. Individual captures do not each need a root-manual entry. Raw captures are dated interview evidence, not automatically current business truth.
- Give a short recap: clickable capture path, context pages actually updated, remaining flags, next step or resume point.

Do not act on the plan until the user confirms you have reached a shared understanding.

## Boundaries and verification

- Scope is the local interview capture and any requested context updates. No global-memory edits, publication, messages, or external-system changes. Do not request or save credentials; use references or redactions for secrets.
- The starter kit ignores `brainstorms/` in Git by default. This does not remove captures already tracked in an existing repository.
- Validate a fresh session, a same-day name collision, a resume, a correcting answer, a stop request, and a failed write. Verify that only confirmed facts reach canonical context and that earlier answers remain traceable.

// 7-module linear bootcamp definition.
//
// Each module has 3 parts: Note, Quiz, Drill. Lessons and drills are tagged
// to modules via the maps below (kept here rather than in-line on each item
// so the verbatim source comments in data/drills.ts and data/lessons.ts stay
// intact). The lookup helpers expose moduleId on a per-item basis.

export type ModuleId = "m1" | "m2" | "m3" | "m4" | "m5" | "m6" | "m7";

export type ModulePart = "note" | "quiz" | "drill";

export type QuizSource =
  | "whyHardItems"
  | "termQuizItems"
  | "translatorItems"
  | "matchingItems"
  | "methodQuizItems"
  | "trapItems"
  | "abstractChoices";

export interface ModuleRecord {
  id: ModuleId;
  number: number;
  name: string;
  subtitle: string;
  noteLessonId: string;
  quizSource: QuizSource;
  quizPassThreshold: number;
  drillIds: string[];
}

export const MODULES: ModuleRecord[] = [
  {
    id: "m1",
    number: 1,
    name: "Why Abstract Is Hard",
    subtitle: "Feel the difficulty before learning the fix.",
    noteLessonId: "why_hard",

    drillIds: ["drill_1", "drill_2"],
  },
  {

    name: "LSAT Words You Must Know",
    subtitle: "Seven terms with LSAT-specific meanings.",
    noteLessonId: "words_to_know",
    quizSource: "termQuizItems",
    quizPassThreshold: 0.6,
    drillIds: ["drill_3", "drill_4"],
  },
  {
    id: "m3",
    number: 3,
    name: "Stimulus + Keyword Extraction",
    subtitle: "The core method. Find the load-bearing words.",
    noteLessonId: "deabstractifying_method",
    quizSource: "translatorItems",
    quizPassThreshold: 0.6,
    drillIds: ["drill_5", "drill_6", "drill_7", "drill_8", "drill_9"],
  },
  {
    id: "m4",
    number: 4,
    name: "Match To The Stimulus",
    subtitle: "Test each part of the answer choice against the stimulus.",
    noteLessonId: "art_of_matching",
    quizSource: "matchingItems",
    quizPassThreshold: 0.6,
    drillIds: ["drill_10", "drill_11", "drill_12", "drill_13", "drill_14"],
  },
  {
    id: "m5",
    number: 5,
    name: "Spot The Method",
    subtitle: "Seventeen method-of-reasoning patterns.",
    noteLessonId: "master_keys",
    quizSource: "methodQuizItems",
    quizPassThreshold: 0.6,
    drillIds: ["drill_15", "drill_16"],
  },
  {
    id: "m6",
    number: 6,
    name: "Catch The Traps",

    noteLessonId: "five_bucket_indicators",

    drillIds: ["drill_17", "drill_18"],
  },
  {

    noteLessonId: "abstract_15",
    quizSource: "abstractChoices",
    quizPassThreshold: 0.6,
    drillIds: ["drill_19", "drill_20"],
  },
];

export function getModule(id: ModuleId): ModuleRecord | undefined {
  return MODULES.find((m) => m.id === id);
}

const DRILL_MODULE_MAP: Record<string, ModuleId> = MODULES.reduce(
  (acc, m) => {
    for (const drillId of m.drillIds) acc[drillId] = m.id;
    return acc;
  },
  {} as Record<string, ModuleId>,
);

const LESSON_MODULE_MAP: Record<string, ModuleId> = MODULES.reduce(
  (acc, m) => {
    acc[m.noteLessonId] = m.id;
    return acc;
  },
  {} as Record<string, ModuleId>,
);

export function moduleIdForDrill(drillId: string): ModuleId | undefined {
  return DRILL_MODULE_MAP[drillId];
}

export function moduleIdForLesson(lessonId: string): ModuleId | undefined {
  return LESSON_MODULE_MAP[lessonId];
}

import { AppProvider, useApp } from "./store/AppContext";
import AppShell from "./components/layout/AppShell";
import Dashboard from "./components/views/Dashboard";
import ModuleView from "./components/views/ModuleView";
import LessonReader from "./components/views/LessonReader";
import DrillRunner from "./components/views/DrillRunner";
import { Flashcards } from "./components/views/Flashcards";
import { MethodsLibrary } from "./components/views/MethodsLibrary";
import TimedFinalSet from "./components/views/TimedFinalSet";
import CompletionReport from "./components/views/CompletionReport";
import "./styles/brutal.css";

function ViewManager() {
  const { currentView } = useApp();

  switch (currentView) {
    case "dashboard":
      return <Dashboard />;
    case "module":
      return <ModuleView />;
    case "lesson":
      return <LessonReader />;
    case "drill":
      return <DrillRunner />;
    case "flashcards":
      return <Flashcards />;
    case "methods_library":
      return <MethodsLibrary />;
    case "timed_set":
      return <TimedFinalSet />;
    case "completion_report":
      return <CompletionReport />;
    default:
      return (
        <div className="flex flex-col items-center justify-center h-full">
          <h2 className="text-xl font-medium mb-2">Work In Progress</h2>
          <p>This section is part of the bootcamp but not fully built in this prototype view.</p>
        </div>
      );
  }
}

// AbstractBootcamp: LSATU mount component for /topics/abstract route.
// Wraps the brutalist bootcamp in a `.topic-abstract` scope so the cream
// paper background and 4px-border aesthetic stays scoped to this surface
// and does not leak into other LSATU dark-mode pages.
export default function AbstractBootcamp() {
  return (
    <div className="topic-abstract h-screen w-full">
      <AppProvider>
        <AppShell>
          <ViewManager />
        </AppShell>
      </AppProvider>
    </div>
  );
}

2026-10-12
25-- **Specific, never generic.** Real scores, the actual skill, the student's own history. Praise is a specific observation, not "great job."
26-- **Honest about the work, gentle about the person.** Be blunt that the LSAT is hard ("expect this to be exhausting and very challenging"). Never be blunt about a student's shortcomings. Honesty points at the material; warmth points at the person. When someone slips, nudge gently, never accuse.
27:- **Light, grounded metaphor, understated in email.** In his real correspondence the recurring image is quiet: "partner with you on your LSAT journey," "getting set up," "foundational." The vivid images (runway, climb, march toward November, an easy door to walk through) belong to authored teaching and brand copy, not to email. Keep metaphor light and grounded; never float off into abstraction.
28-- **Balanced rhythm.** Air between ideas. Real correspondence is short and single-purpose (median ~280 characters). In teaching, vary sentence and paragraph length with the reasoning: give an explanation room, then let a short sentence settle the consequence. Do not impose email brevity on a lesson or cut its intermediate reasoning.
29-- **Plain professional diction.** No corporate jargon, no startup hype ("unlock," "crush," "level up," "game-changer"; "crush" appears once in 944 messages). Contractions are the norm, not the exception. Exclamation points are genuine, not performed: a warm message often carries one, occasionally more, especially in openers ("Sorry for such a long delay!"). He is not exclamation-averse.
30-- **Forward-looking.** End on the next step or what's ahead.
31-- **No em dashes, ever.** Use periods, colons before lists, the middot (·) as a separator, and numbered steps for procedures.

    <h4 className="text-xs font-black uppercase tracking-widest mb-3 text-brutal-ink">{title}</h4>

);

## assistant

Saving the session to [brainstorms/2026-10-12-abstract-bootcamp-fix.md](brainstorms/2026-10-12-abstract-bootcamp-fix.md).

What I found: Abstract has 7 modules (Why Abstract Is Hard → Words You Must Know → Keyword Extraction → Match to the Stimulus → Spot the Method → Catch the Traps → Put It Together), 20 drills, flashcards, a methods library and a timed final set. It uses its own yellow neo-brutalist look. Most lesson "practice" is Reveal-button cycling, which the Contrapositive standard rejects. It's live. I'm checking separately whether any students have saved progress in it.

**Round 1**

**Q1 - What "fix" means**: Which of these do you mean?
(a) Rebuild every lesson note to the Contrapositive standard: plain definition first, one example plus one fresh one, a demo the student drives, "what it is not", a Pop Quiz. Quizzes and drills keep their questions but get the new feedback style.
(b) Copy and wording only, keeping the current pages and interactions.
(c) A full redesign, including whether all seven modules should stay.

Recommended: (a). The structure is reasonable. The weak parts are the teaching and the reveal-button practice.

---

**Q2 - Look**: Should Abstract keep its yellow neo-brutalist style, or move to the shared LSAT U lesson look used by Intro to LR and Conditionals?

Recommended: Move to the shared look. Uppercase italic body text with heavy borders is hard to read in long notes, and students shouldn't feel like they've switched products between bootcamps.

---

**Q3 - First page to rebuild**: The usual process is to rebuild one page, get your approval, then roll the pattern out. Which page goes first?

Recommended: Module 3, Keyword Extraction. It teaches the core method that every later module uses, so locking its wording and demo first sets the pattern for the rest.

---

**Q4 - Name**: The Library lists it as just "Abstract". Keep that, or rename it to something like "Abstract Answer Choices"?

Recommended: "Abstract Answer Choices". "Abstract" alone doesn't tell a student what they'll practice, and you recently rejected titles for the same reason.

---

**Q5 - Sources**: Should the rebuild draw on `corpus/Curriculum/Abstract` (parsing worksheets, answer keys, the Translation and Cluster Sentences quiz), the module's existing source notes, or something else you have in mind, such as your session transcripts or an LSAT Lab or Demon lesson?

Recommended: Corpus plus the existing source notes, with fresh examples written for the page, as we did for Conditionals.

export type ConfidenceLevel = 'Guessing' | 'Unsure' | 'Pretty sure' | 'Confident';

export type ViewState = 'dashboard' | 'drill' | 'completion_report' | 'lesson' | 'flashcards' | 'methods_library' | 'mixed_practice' | 'timed_final_set' | 'timed_set' | 'module';

export type ModulePartName = 'note' | 'quiz' | 'drill';

export type MasteryLevel = 'none' | 'practicing' | 'solid' | 'mastered';

export interface ModuleProgressRow {
  moduleId: string;
  noteComplete: boolean;
  quizScore: number;
  quizTotal: number;
  quizStreakHigh: number;
  drillComplete: boolean;
  drillCorrect?: number;
  drillTotal?: number;
  aced: boolean;
  masteryLevel: MasteryLevel;
  completedAt?: string;
}

export type DrillType = 
  | 'keyword_extraction'
  | 'term_definition'
  | 'translation'
  | 'matching'
  | 'one_word_trap'
  | 'role'
  | 'method'
  | 'flaw'
  | 'mixed';

export interface CardReview {
  cardId: string;
  status: 'unseen' | 'seen' | 'reviewing' | 'mastered';
  lastReviewedAt?: string;
  reviewCount: number;
  // SRS bookkeeping. Optional so legacy localStorage records stay readable;
  // missing fields imply a brand-new card scheduled to be due immediately.
  rating?: 'again' | 'hard' | 'good' | 'easy';
  intervalDays?: number;
  dueAtIso?: string;
}

export interface Flashcard {
  id: string;
  question: string;
  answer: string;
  category?: string;
}

export interface MethodPattern {
  id: string;
  name: string;
  plainEnglish: string;
  answerChoiceLanguage: string;
  mustHappen: string;
  example: {
    stimulus: string;
    explanation: string;
  };
}

// DrillAttempt: per-attempt record retained client-side for the Drill view
// and CompletionReport. Mirrors the abstract_drill_attempts row written to
// Supabase, minus the server-side id/user_id columns.
export interface DrillAttempt {
  drillId: string;
  drillType: DrillType;
  selectedIndex: number;
  correct: boolean;
  confidence: ConfidenceLevel;
  trapTag?: string;
  // Response time in ms (from stimulus visible to Submit click). Optional
  // so legacy attempts without instrumentation can hydrate without backfill.
  timeMs?: number;
  completedAt: string;
}

// GauntletAttempt: client-side snapshot of an abstract_gauntlet_attempts row.
export interface GauntletAttempt {
  choiceId: number;
  keywordsPicked: string[];
  translationText: string;
  requirementsChecked: number[];
  correctCount: number;
  completedAt: string;
}

// TranslatorAttempt: client-side snapshot of an abstract_translator_attempts row.
// Used by the 14-stem question-stem translation drill.
export interface TranslatorAttempt {
  stemNumber: number;
  keywordsPicked: string[];
  translationText: string;
  completedAt: string;
}

export interface TranslatorVocabCallout {
  term: string;
  meaning: string;
  note?: string;
}

export interface TranslatorWalkthrough {
  reasoning: string[];
  vocab: TranslatorVocabCallout[];
  requirements: string[];
  example: { stimulus: string; explanation: string };
}

export interface TranslatorDrillItem {
  stemNumber: number;
  stemText: string;
  correctKeywords: string[];
  idealTranslation: string;
  walkthrough: TranslatorWalkthrough;
}

export interface BootcampState {
  completedLessons: string[];
  completedDrills: string[];
  drillAttempts: DrillAttempt[];
  gauntletAttempts: GauntletAttempt[];
  translatorAttempts: TranslatorAttempt[];
  cards: Record<string, CardReview>;
  moduleProgress: Record<string, ModuleProgressRow>;
  xpTotal: number;
  dailyStreak: number;
  dailyStreakLastActive?: string;
  aceBadges: string[];
  finalSetScore?: {
    score: number;
    total: number;
    completedAt: string;
  };
  drillsStats: {
    [key in DrillType]?: {
      correct: number;
      total: number;
    }
  };
}

export interface Lesson {
  id: string;
  title: string;
  content: string; // Markdown or plain text
  type: 'concept' | 'interactive';
  hasInteractiveContent?: boolean;
}

export interface KeywordExtractionItem {
  id: string;
  answerChoice: string;
  keywords: {
    text: string;
    type: 'noun' | 'verb' | 'qualifier' | 'relationship';
    definition: string;
  }[];
}

export interface MatchingItem {
  id: string;
  stimulus: string;
  answerChoice: string;
  parts: {
    text: string;
    matches: boolean;
    explanation: string;
    highlightText?: string;
  }[];
}

export interface TermDefinitionItem {
  id: string;
  term: string;
  options: string[];
  correctOption: number;
  definition: string;
  explanation: string;
}

export interface DrillOption {
  text: string;
  explanation: string;
}

export interface DrillItem {
  id: string;
  type: DrillType;
  stimulus?: string;
  question?: string;
  options: DrillOption[];
  correctOptionIndex: number;
  explanation: string;
  trapTag?: string;
}

export interface AbstractChoice {
  id: number;
  original: string;
  correctKeywords: string[];
  distractorKeywords: string[];
  plainEnglish: string;
  stimulusRequirements: string[];
  trapRequirements: string[];
  coachNote: string;
  example?: string;
  structureType?: string;
}

// BootcampMutationResult: standard LSATU {ok}|{ok:false,error} contract.
// Mirrors the AttemptResult shape used by hooks like useRecordChapterSection
// so callers can branch on .ok without learning a new pattern.
//
// Expressed as a single-shape interface (error optional) rather than a
// discriminated union because strictNullChecks is off in this project and
// the union-narrowing of !res.ok is unreliable under that config. Callers
// still write `if (!res.ok) { use res.error }` which works correctly at
// runtime; the type just admits res.error in both branches.
export interface BootcampMutationResult {
  ok: boolean;
  error?: unknown;
}

export interface QuestionStem {
  id: string;
  category: 'Role' | 'Method' | 'Flaw' | 'Other';
  stem: string;
  explanation: string;
}

export interface FinalSetChoice {
  letter: 'A' | 'B' | 'C' | 'D' | 'E';
  text: string;
}

export interface FinalSetQuestion {
  id: string;
  ptRef: string;
  stimulus: string;
  questionStem: string;
  choices: FinalSetChoice[];
  correctLetter: 'A' | 'B' | 'C' | 'D' | 'E';
  explanation: string;
}

172	function ModuleOverview({
173	  moduleId,
174	  onPick,
175	}: {
176	  moduleId: ModuleId;
177	  onPick: (stage: Stage) => void;
178	}) {
179	  const { bootcampState, isPartUnlocked } = useApp();
180	  const mod = getModule(moduleId)!;
181	  const row = bootcampState.moduleProgress[moduleId];
182	  const noteDone = Boolean(row?.noteComplete);
183	  const quizDone = row?.quizTotal > 0 && row.quizScore / row.quizTotal >= mod.quizPassThreshold;
184	  const drillDone = Boolean(row?.drillComplete);
185	  const mastery: MasteryLevel = row?.masteryLevel ?? "none";
186	
187	  return (
188	    <>
189	      <div className="mb-6">
190	        <p className="text-xs font-black uppercase tracking-[0.2em] text-brutal-ink-soft mb-1">
191	          Module {mod.number}
192	        </p>
193	        <h1 className="text-3xl md:text-4xl font-black uppercase tracking-tight leading-tight">
194	          {mod.name}
195	        </h1>
196	        <p className="font-bold text-brutal-ink-soft mt-3">{mod.subtitle}</p>
197	        {mastery !== "none" && (
198	          <div className="mt-3">
199	            <MasteryBadge level={mastery} />
200	          </div>
201	        )}
202	      </div>
203	
204	      <div className="space-y-4">
205	        <PartRow
206	          icon={BookOpen}
207	          label="Note"
208	          meta="Read the lesson"
209	          status={noteDone ? "done" : isPartUnlocked(moduleId, "note") ? "active" : "locked"}
210	          accent="cyan"
211	          onOpen={() => onPick("note")}
212	          ctaLabel={noteDone ? "Re-read" : "Start"}
213	        />
214	        <PartRow
215	          icon={ListChecks}
216	          label="Quiz"
217	          meta={
218	            row?.quizTotal
219	              ? `${row.quizScore} of ${row.quizTotal} correct`
220	              : `${quizItemCount(mod.quizSource)} items`
221	          }
222	          status={quizDone ? "done" : isPartUnlocked(moduleId, "quiz") ? "active" : "locked"}
223	          lockedHint="Read the note to unlock."
224	          accent="yellow"
225	          onOpen={() => onPick("quiz")}
226	          ctaLabel={quizDone ? "Retake" : "Start"}
227	        />
228	        <PartRow
229	          icon={Target}
230	          label="Drill"
231	          meta={`${mod.drillIds.length} LSAT questions`}
232	          status={drillDone ? "done" : isPartUnlocked(moduleId, "drill") ? "active" : "locked"}
233	          lockedHint={`Pass the quiz at ${Math.round(mod.quizPassThreshold * 100)}% to unlock.`}
234	          accent="pink"
235	          onOpen={() => onPick("drill")}
236	          ctaLabel={drillDone ? "Retake" : "Start"}
237	        />
238	      </div>
239	    </>
240	  );
241	}
242	
243	function PartRow({
244	  icon: Icon,
245	  label,
246	  meta,
247	  status,
248	  lockedHint,
249	  accent,
250	  onOpen,
251	  ctaLabel,
252	}: {
253	  icon: typeof BookOpen;
254	  label: string;
255	  meta: string;
256	  status: "done" | "active" | "locked";
257	  lockedHint?: string;
258	  accent: "cyan" | "yellow" | "pink";
259	  onOpen: () => void;
260	  ctaLabel: string;
261	}) {
262	  const accentBg =
263	    accent === "cyan" ? "bg-brutal-cyan" : accent === "yellow" ? "bg-brutal-yellow" : "bg-brutal-pink";
264	  if (status === "done") {
265	    return (
266	      <div className="bg-brutal-paper border-4 border-brutal-ink p-4 flex items-center gap-4">
267	        <CheckCircle2 className="w-6 h-6 shrink-0" />
268	        <div className="flex-1">
269	          <p className="font-black uppercase tracking-tight">{label}</p>
270	          <p className="text-xs font-bold text-brutal-ink-soft">{meta}</p>
271	        </div>
272	        <button
273	          type="button"
274	          onClick={onOpen}
275	          className="text-xs font-black uppercase tracking-wider underline"
276	        >
277	          {ctaLabel}
278	        </button>
279	      </div>
280	    );
281	  }
282	  if (status === "locked") {
283	    return (
284	      <div className="bg-brutal-paper border-4 border-brutal-ink-trace p-6 flex items-center gap-4 opacity-70">
285	        <Lock className="w-6 h-6 shrink-0" />
286	        <div className="flex-1">
287	          <p className="font-black uppercase tracking-tight">{label}</p>
288	          <p className="text-xs font-bold text-brutal-ink-soft">{lockedHint ?? "Locked"}</p>
289	        </div>
290	      </div>
291	    );
292	  }
293	  return (
294	    <button
295	      type="button"
296	      onClick={onOpen}
297	      className={cn(
298	        "w-full text-left p-6 border-4 border-brutal-ink brutal-shadow transition-shadow",
299	        accentBg,
300	        "hover:translate-x-[-2px] hover:translate-y-[-2px]",
301	      )}
302	    >
303	      <div className="flex items-center gap-4">
304	        <Icon className="w-8 h-8 shrink-0" />
305	        <div className="flex-1">
306	          <p className="text-2xl font-black uppercase tracking-tight">{label}</p>
307	          <p className="text-sm font-bold text-brutal-ink-soft mt-1">{meta}</p>
308	        </div>
309	        <span className="inline-flex items-center gap-2 text-xs font-black uppercase tracking-wider">
310	          {ctaLabel} <ArrowRight className="w-4 h-4" />
311	        </span>
312	      </div>
313	    </button>
314	  );
315	}
316	
317	function quizItemCount(source: QuizSource): number {
318	  switch (source) {
319	    case "whyHardItems":
320	      return WHY_HARD_ITEMS.length;
321	    case "termQuizItems":
322	      return TERM_QUIZ_ITEMS.length;
323	    case "translatorItems":
324	      return TRANSLATOR_ITEMS.length;
325	    case "matchingItems":
326	      return MATCHING_ITEMS.length;
327	    case "methodQuizItems":
328	      return METHOD_QUIZ_ITEMS.length;
329	    case "trapItems":
330	      return TRAP_ITEMS.length;
331	    case "abstractChoices":
332	      return ABSTRACT_CHOICES.length;
333	  }
334	}
335	
336	// ============ Note ============
337	
338	function ModuleNote({
339	  moduleId,
340	  onDone,
341	  onStartQuiz,
342	}: {
343	  moduleId: ModuleId;
344	  onDone: () => void;
345	  onStartQuiz: () => void;
346	}) {
347	  const { markNoteRead, bootcampState } = useApp();
348	  const mod = getModule(moduleId)!;
349	  const lesson = LESSONS.find((l) => l.id === mod.noteLessonId);
350	  const sentinelRef = useRef<HTMLDivElement>(null);
351	  const [reachedEnd, setReachedEnd] = useState(false);
352	  const [marked, setMarked] = useState(
353	    Boolean(bootcampState.moduleProgress[moduleId]?.noteComplete),
354	  );
355	  const [pulse, setPulse] = useState(false);
356	
357	  // Read-aware enable: watch a sentinel placed right after the lesson
358	  // body. The button activates once the sentinel comes into view inside
359	  // the scrolling ancestor (AppShell's main area). Walking up the DOM to
360	  // find the scroll container lets us use IntersectionObserver with that
361	  // ancestor as root, which behaves correctly when the page itself
362	  // scrolls rather than the lesson card.
363	  useEffect(() => {
364	    const el = sentinelRef.current;
365	    if (!el) return;
366	    let parent: HTMLElement | null = el.parentElement;
367	    let root: HTMLElement | null = null;
368	    while (parent) {
369	      const style = window.getComputedStyle(parent);
370	      const overflowY = style.overflowY;
371	      if (overflowY === "auto" || overflowY === "scroll") {
372	        root = parent;
373	        break;
374	      }
375	      parent = parent.parentElement;
376	    }
377	    const obs = new IntersectionObserver(
378	      (entries) => {
379	        for (const entry of entries) {
380	          if (entry.isIntersecting) {
381	            setReachedEnd((prev) => {
382	              if (!prev) {
383	                setPulse(true);
384	                setTimeout(() => setPulse(false), 800);
385	              }
386	              return true;
387	            });
388	          }
389	        }
390	      },
391	      { root, rootMargin: "0px 0px -120px 0px", threshold: 0 },
392	    );
393	    obs.observe(el);
394	    return () => obs.disconnect();
395	  }, []);
396	
397	  const onMark = async () => {
398	    await markNoteRead(moduleId);
399	    setMarked(true);
400	  };
401	
402	  return (
403	    <div>
404	      <h2 className="text-2xl font-black uppercase tracking-tight mb-2">
405	        Note: {lesson?.title ?? mod.name}
406	      </h2>
407	      <p className="font-bold text-brutal-ink-soft mb-6">{mod.subtitle}</p>
408	
409	      <div className="bg-brutal-paper border-4 border-brutal-ink brutal-shadow mb-6">
410	        <div className="px-6 md:px-10 py-8 md:py-12">
411	          <LessonBody lessonId={mod.noteLessonId} />
412	        </div>
413	      </div>
414	      <div ref={sentinelRef} aria-hidden="true" className="h-px w-full" />
415	
416	      <AnimatePresence mode="wait">
417	        {!marked ? (
418	          <motion.div
419	            key="read-row"
420	            initial={false}
421	            exit={{ opacity: 0, y: -4 }}
422	            transition={{ duration: 0.24, ease: [0.22, 1, 0.36, 1] }}
423	            className="flex items-center gap-3 flex-wrap"
424	          >
425	            <button
426	              type="button"
427	              onClick={onMark}
428	              className={cn(
429	                "brutal-btn inline-flex items-center gap-2 bg-brutal-green",
430	                pulse && "animate-pulse",
431	              )}
432	              title="Mark this note as read"
433	            >
434	              <CheckCircle2 className="w-4 h-4" /> I read this
435	            </button>
436	            <motion.span
437	              initial={{ opacity: 0, x: -4 }}
438	              animate={{ opacity: 1, x: 0 }}
439	              transition={{ duration: 0.24, ease: [0.22, 1, 0.36, 1] }}
440	              className="inline-flex items-center gap-1 text-xs font-black uppercase tracking-widest bg-brutal-yellow border-2 border-brutal-ink px-3 py-1.5"
441	            >
442	              {reachedEnd ? "Ready" : "Click when done"}
443	            </motion.span>
444	          </motion.div>
445	        ) : (
446	          <motion.div
447	            key="quiz-cta"
448	            initial={{ opacity: 0, y: 8 }}
449	            animate={{ opacity: 1, y: 0 }}
450	            transition={{ duration: 0.32, ease: [0.22, 1, 0.36, 1] }}
451	            className="flex items-center gap-3 flex-wrap"
452	          >
453	            <button
454	              type="button"
455	              onClick={onStartQuiz}
456	              className="brutal-btn bg-brutal-green inline-flex items-center gap-2"
457	            >
458	              Start quiz <ArrowRight className="w-4 h-4" />
459	            </button>
460	            <span className="text-xs font-black uppercase tracking-widest text-brutal-ink-soft">
461	              Quiz unlocked
462	            </span>
463	          </motion.div>
464	        )}
465	      </AnimatePresence>
466	    </div>
467	  );
468	}
469	
470	// ============ Quiz ============
471	
472	type QuizVerdict = "correct" | "partial" | "incorrect";
473	
474	function ModuleQuiz({ moduleId, onDone }: { moduleId: ModuleId; onDone: () => void }) {
475	  const { recordQuizScore, addXp } = useApp();
476	  const mod = getModule(moduleId)!;
477	  const items = useMemo(() => loadQuizItems(mod.quizSource), [mod.quizSource]);
478	  const [index, setIndex] = useState(0);
479	  const [score, setScore] = useState(0); // can be fractional via partial credit
480	  const [streak, setStreak] = useState(0);
481	  const [streakHigh, setStreakHigh] = useState(0);
482	  const [finished, setFinished] = useState(false);
483	
484	  if (finished) {
485	    const passed = items.length > 0 && score / items.length >= mod.quizPassThreshold;
486	    const displayScore = Number.isInteger(score) ? score : score.toFixed(1);
487	    return (
488	      <div className="text-center">
489	        <h2 className="text-3xl font-black uppercase tracking-tight mb-4">Quiz complete</h2>
490	        <div className="inline-block bg-brutal-yellow border-4 border-brutal-ink p-6 brutal-shadow mb-6">
491	          <p className="text-6xl font-black">
492	            {displayScore} / {items.length}
493	          </p>
494	          <p className="text-xs font-black uppercase tracking-widest mt-2">Correct</p>
495	        </div>
496	        {streakHigh >= 3 && (
497	          <p className="font-bold mb-4 inline-flex items-center gap-2">
498	            <Flame className="w-4 h-4" /> Best run: {streakHigh} in a row
499	          </p>
500	        )}
501	        <p className="font-bold mb-6">
502	          {passed ? "Drill unlocked. Nice. Next." : `Need ${Math.round(mod.quizPassThreshold * 100)}% to unlock the drill. Retake when ready.`}
503	        </p>
504	        <button
505	          type="button"
506	          onClick={onDone}
507	          className="brutal-btn bg-brutal-green inline-flex items-center gap-2"
508	        >
509	          Continue <ArrowRight className="w-4 h-4" />
510	        </button>
511	      </div>
512	    );
513	  }
514	
515	  const onAnswer = (verdictOrCorrect: boolean | QuizVerdict) => {
516	    const verdict: QuizVerdict =
517	      typeof verdictOrCorrect === "boolean"
518	        ? verdictOrCorrect
519	          ? "correct"
520	          : "incorrect"
521	        : verdictOrCorrect;
522	    if (verdict === "correct") {
523	      setScore((s) => s + 1);
524	      setStreak((s) => {
525	        const next = s + 1;
526	        setStreakHigh((h) => Math.max(h, next));
527	        return next;
528	      });
529	      addXp(1);
530	    } else if (verdict === "partial") {
531	      setScore((s) => s + 0.5);
532	      setStreak(0);
533	    } else {
534	      setStreak(0);
535	    }
536	  };
537	
538	  const onNext = () => {
539	    if (index + 1 >= items.length) {
540	      setFinished(true);
541	      void recordQuizScore(moduleId, Math.round(score), items.length, streakHigh);
542	    } else {
543	      setIndex((i) => i + 1);
544	    }
545	  };
546	
547	  const progressPct = ((index) / items.length) * 100;
548	  return (
549	    <div>
550	      <div className="flex items-center justify-between mb-3 gap-3">
551	        <div className="flex-1">
552	          <div className="flex items-center justify-between mb-1.5">
553	            <p className="text-[10px] font-black uppercase tracking-widest text-brutal-ink-soft">
554	              Item {index + 1} of {items.length}
555	            </p>
556	            <KeyboardHelp />
557	          </div>
558	          <div className="h-2 bg-brutal-ink-trace border border-brutal-ink">
559	            <div
560	              className="h-full bg-brutal-ink transition-[width] duration-300"
561	              style={{ width: `${progressPct}%` }}
562	            />
563	          </div>
564	        </div>
565	        {streak >= 3 && (
566	          <p className="inline-flex items-center gap-1 text-xs font-black uppercase tracking-wider shrink-0">
567	            <Flame className="w-4 h-4" /> {streak}
568	          </p>
569	        )}
570	      </div>
571	      <QuizItemRenderer
572	        key={index}
573	        source={mod.quizSource}
574	        item={items[index]}
575	        onAnswer={onAnswer}
576	        onNext={onNext}
577	      />
578	    </div>
579	  );
580	}
581	
582	function KeyboardHelp() {
583	  const [open, setOpen] = useState(false);
584	  return (
585	    <span className="relative">
586	      <button
587	        type="button"
588	        aria-label="Keyboard shortcuts"
589	        onMouseEnter={() => setOpen(true)}
590	        onMouseLeave={() => setOpen(false)}
591	        onFocus={() => setOpen(true)}
592	        onBlur={() => setOpen(false)}
593	        className="text-brutal-ink-soft hover:text-brutal-ink"
594	      >
595	        <HelpCircle className="w-4 h-4" />
596	      </button>
597	      {open && (
598	        <span className="absolute right-0 top-full mt-1 w-60 bg-brutal-paper border-2 border-brutal-ink p-3 z-50 text-left text-xs font-bold normal-case tracking-normal pointer-events-none">
599	          <span className="block text-[10px] font-black uppercase tracking-widest mb-1.5">
600	            Keyboard
601	          </span>
602	          <span className="block">1 - 5: pick option</span>
603	          <span className="block">Enter: submit</span>
604	          <span className="block">Space: next</span>
605	        </span>
606	      )}
607	    </span>
608	  );
609	}
610	
611	function loadQuizItems(source: QuizSource): unknown[] {
612	  switch (source) {
613	    case "whyHardItems":
614	      return WHY_HARD_ITEMS;
615	    case "termQuizItems":
616	      return TERM_QUIZ_ITEMS;
617	    case "translatorItems":
618	      return TRANSLATOR_ITEMS;
619	    case "matchingItems":
620	      return MATCHING_ITEMS;
621	    case "methodQuizItems":
622	      return METHOD_QUIZ_ITEMS;
623	    case "trapItems":
624	      return TRAP_ITEMS;
625	    case "abstractChoices":
626	      return ABSTRACT_CHOICES;
627	  }
628	}
629	
630	function QuizItemRenderer({
631	  source,
632	  item,
633	  onAnswer,
634	  onNext,
635	}: {
636	  source: QuizSource;
637	  item: unknown;
638	  onAnswer: (verdict: boolean | QuizVerdict) => void;
639	  onNext: () => void;
640	}) {
641	  switch (source) {
642	    case "whyHardItems":
643	      return <WhyHardRenderer item={item as typeof WHY_HARD_ITEMS[number]} onAnswer={onAnswer} onNext={onNext} />;
644	    case "termQuizItems":
645	      return <TermQuizRenderer item={item as typeof TERM_QUIZ_ITEMS[number]} onAnswer={onAnswer} onNext={onNext} />;
646	    case "translatorItems":
647	      return <KeywordExtractionRenderer item={item as typeof TRANSLATOR_ITEMS[number]} onAnswer={onAnswer} onNext={onNext} />;
648	    case "matchingItems":
649	      return <MatchToStimulusRenderer item={item as typeof MATCHING_ITEMS[number]} onAnswer={onAnswer} onNext={onNext} />;
650	    case "methodQuizItems":
651	      return <MethodPickerRenderer item={item as typeof METHOD_QUIZ_ITEMS[number]} onAnswer={onAnswer} onNext={onNext} />;
652	    case "trapItems":
653	      return <TrapSpotterRenderer item={item as typeof TRAP_ITEMS[number]} onAnswer={onAnswer} onNext={onNext} />;
654	    case "abstractChoices":
655	      return <DecodingGauntletRenderer item={item as typeof ABSTRACT_CHOICES[number]} onAnswer={onAnswer} onNext={onNext} />;
656	  }
657	}
658	
659	// Generic single-column MC renderer used by most quiz sources.
660	function MultipleChoiceCard({
661	  prompt,
662	  context,
663	  options,
664	  correctIndex,
665	  explanation,
666	  onAnswer,
667	  onNext,
668	}: {
669	  prompt: string;
670	  context?: React.ReactNode;
671	  options: string[];
672	  correctIndex: number;
673	  explanation: string;
674	  onAnswer: (verdict: boolean | QuizVerdict) => void;
675	  onNext: () => void;
676	}) {
677	  const { autoAdvance } = useApp();
678	  const [picked, setPicked] = useState<number | null>(null);
679	  const [eliminated, setEliminated] = useState<Set<number>>(new Set());
680	  const [showHint, setShowHint] = useState(false);
681	  const [hintUsed, setHintUsed] = useState(false);
682	  const [paused, setPaused] = useState(false);
683	  const submitted = picked !== null;
684	  const wasCorrect = picked === correctIndex;
685	
686	  useEffect(() => {
687	    if (submitted) return;
688	    const t = setTimeout(() => setShowHint(true), 30000);
689	    return () => clearTimeout(t);
690	  }, [submitted]);
691	
692	  const pick = (i: number) => {
693	    if (submitted) return;
694	    if (eliminated.has(i)) return;
695	    setPicked(i);
696	    const isCorrect = i === correctIndex;
697	    const verdict: QuizVerdict = hintUsed
698	      ? (isCorrect ? "partial" : "incorrect")
699	      : (isCorrect ? "correct" : "incorrect");
700	    onAnswer(verdict);
701	  };
702	
703	  useEffect(() => {
704	    const onKey = (e: KeyboardEvent) => {
705	      if (e.target instanceof HTMLInputElement || e.target instanceof HTMLTextAreaElement) return;
706	      if (!submitted) {
707	        const n = parseInt(e.key, 10);
708	        if (!Number.isNaN(n) && n >= 1 && n <= options.length) {
709	          e.preventDefault();
710	          pick(n - 1);
711	        }
712	      } else if (e.key === " " || e.key === "Enter") {
713	        e.preventDefault();
714	        onNext();
715	      }
716	    };
717	    window.addEventListener("keydown", onKey);
718	    return () => window.removeEventListener("keydown", onKey);
719	    // eslint-disable-next-line react-hooks/exhaustive-deps
720	  }, [submitted, options.length]);
721	
722	  useEffect(() => {
723	    if (!submitted || !wasCorrect || !autoAdvance || paused) return;
724	    const t = setTimeout(onNext, 1200);
725	    return () => clearTimeout(t);
726	  }, [submitted, wasCorrect, autoAdvance, paused, onNext]);
727	
728	  const useHint = () => {
729	    setHintUsed(true);
730	    setShowHint(false);
731	    for (let i = 0; i < options.length; i++) {
732	      if (i !== correctIndex && !eliminated.has(i)) {
733	        setEliminated(new Set([...eliminated, i]));
734	        break;
735	      }
736	    }
737	  };
738	
739	  return (
740	    <div className="bg-brutal-paper border-4 border-brutal-ink p-6 brutal-shadow">
741	      {context && <div className="mb-5">{context}</div>}
742	      <p className="font-black mb-4">{prompt}</p>
743	      <ul className="space-y-3 list-none m-0 p-0">
744	        {options.map((opt, i) => {
745	          const isCorrect = i === correctIndex;
746	          const isPicked = picked === i;
747	          const isEliminated = eliminated.has(i);
748	          const tone = !submitted
749	            ? isEliminated
750	              ? "bg-white opacity-30 line-through"
751	              : "bg-white hover:bg-brutal-yellow/30"
752	            : isCorrect
753	              ? "bg-brutal-green"
754	              : isPicked
755	                ? "bg-brutal-red text-brutal-paper"
756	                : "bg-white opacity-60";
757	          return (
758	            <li key={i}>
759	              <button
760	                type="button"
761	                disabled={submitted || isEliminated}
762	                onClick={() => pick(i)}
763	                className={cn(
764	                  "w-full text-left p-4 border-2 border-brutal-ink font-bold transition-colors",
765	                  tone,
766	                )}
767	              >
768	                <span className="text-brutal-ink-soft mr-2 text-xs">{i + 1}.</span>
769	                {opt}
770	              </button>
771	            </li>
772	          );
773	        })}
774	      </ul>
775	      {!submitted && showHint && (
776	        <button
777	          type="button"
778	          onClick={useHint}
779	          className="mt-4 inline-flex items-center gap-2 text-xs font-black uppercase tracking-wider bg-brutal-yellow border-2 border-brutal-ink px-3 py-2"
780	        >
781	          <Lightbulb className="w-3.5 h-3.5" /> Need a hint?
782	        </button>
783	      )}
784	      {submitted && (
785	        <div className="mt-5 bg-brutal-cyan/30 border-2 border-brutal-ink p-4">
786	          {/*
787	            The verdict in words. Its two siblings in this file
788	            (KeywordExtractionRenderer, DecodingGauntletRenderer) both render this
789	            and this one did not, so the only signal that you got it wrong was
790	            brutal-green vs brutal-red fill — a pair that is not separable under
791	            deuteranopia. The "Why" copy below explains the answer but never says
792	            whether you got it.
793	          */}
794	          <div className="mb-3">
795	            <VerdictHeader
796	              verdict={
797	                picked === correctIndex ? (hintUsed ? "partial" : "correct") : "incorrect"
798	              }
799	            />
800	          </div>
801	          <p className="text-xs font-black uppercase tracking-widest mb-2">Why</p>
802	          <p className="font-bold">{explanation}</p>
803	          {hintUsed && (
804	            <p className="text-xs italic mt-2 text-brutal-ink-soft">
805	              Hint used: counted as partial credit.
806	            </p>
807	          )}
808	          <div className="mt-4 flex items-center gap-3">
809	            <button
810	              type="button"
811	              onClick={onNext}
812	              className="brutal-btn bg-brutal-yellow inline-flex items-center gap-2"
813	            >
814	              Next <ArrowRight className="w-4 h-4" />
815	            </button>
816	            {wasCorrect && autoAdvance && (
817	              <button
818	                type="button"
819	                onClick={() => setPaused((p) => !p)}
820	                className="inline-flex items-center gap-1 text-xs font-black uppercase tracking-wider"
821	                aria-label={paused ? "Resume auto-advance" : "Pause auto-advance"}
822	              >
823	                {paused ? <Play className="w-3.5 h-3.5" /> : <Pause className="w-3.5 h-3.5" />}
824	                {paused ? "Resume" : "Pause"}
825	              </button>
826	            )}
827	          </div>
828	          {wasCorrect && autoAdvance && !paused && (
829	            <div className="mt-3 h-1 bg-brutal-ink-trace border border-brutal-ink overflow-hidden">
830	              <motion.div
831	                className="h-full bg-brutal-ink"
832	                initial={{ width: "0%" }}
833	                animate={{ width: "100%" }}
834	                transition={{ duration: 1.2, ease: "linear" }}
835	              />
836	            </div>
837	          )}
838	        </div>
839	      )}
840	    </div>
841	  );
842	}
843	
844	function WhyHardRenderer({
845	  item,
846	  onAnswer,
847	  onNext,
848	}: {
849	  item: typeof WHY_HARD_ITEMS[number];
850	  onAnswer: (verdict: boolean | QuizVerdict) => void;
851	  onNext: () => void;
852	}) {
853	  const correctIdx = item.options.findIndex((o) => o.correct);
854	  return (
855	    <MultipleChoiceCard
856	      prompt={item.question}
857	      context={
858	        <div className="space-y-3">
859	          <div className="bg-white border-2 border-brutal-ink p-3 text-sm font-bold whitespace-pre-line">
860	            {item.stimulus}
861	          </div>
862	          <div className="bg-brutal-yellow border-2 border-brutal-ink p-3 italic font-bold">
863	            {item.answerChoice}
864	          </div>
865	        </div>
866	      }
867	      options={item.options.map((o) => o.text)}
868	      correctIndex={correctIdx}
869	      explanation={item.options[correctIdx]?.explanation ?? ""}
870	      onAnswer={onAnswer}
871	      onNext={onNext}
872	    />
873	  );
874	}
875	
876	function TermQuizRenderer({
877	  item,
878	  onAnswer,
879	  onNext,
880	}: {
881	  item: typeof TERM_QUIZ_ITEMS[number];
882	  onAnswer: (verdict: boolean | QuizVerdict) => void;

884	}) {

886	    <MultipleChoiceCard
887	      prompt={item.prompt}

890	      explanation={item.explanation}
891	      onAnswer={onAnswer}
892	      onNext={onNext}
893	    />
894	  );
895	}
896	
897	function MethodPickerRenderer({
898	  item,
899	  onAnswer,
900	  onNext,
901	}: {
902	  item: typeof METHOD_QUIZ_ITEMS[number];
903	  onAnswer: (verdict: boolean | QuizVerdict) => void;
904	  onNext: () => void;
905	}) {
906	  return (
907	    <MultipleChoiceCard
908	      prompt={item.prompt}
909	      context={
910	        <div className="bg-white border-2 border-brutal-ink p-4 text-sm font-bold whitespace-pre-line">
911	          {item.stimulus}
912	        </div>
913	      }
914	      options={item.options}
915	      correctIndex={item.correctOption}
916	      explanation={item.explanation}
917	      onAnswer={onAnswer}
918	      onNext={onNext}

922	
923	function TrapSpotterRenderer({
924	  item,
925	  onAnswer,
926	  onNext,
927	}: {
928	  item: typeof TRAP_ITEMS[number];
929	  onAnswer: (verdict: boolean | QuizVerdict) => void;
930	  onNext: () => void;
931	}) {
932	  return (
933	    <MultipleChoiceCard
934	      prompt={item.prompt}
935	      context={
936	        <div className="space-y-3">
937	          <div className="bg-white border-2 border-brutal-ink p-3 text-sm font-bold whitespace-pre-line">
938	            {item.stimulus}
939	          </div>
940	          <div className="bg-brutal-red/20 border-2 border-brutal-ink p-3 italic font-bold">
941	            Wrong answer: {item.wrongChoice}
942	          </div>
943	        </div>
944	      }
945	      options={TRAP_OPTION_TEXT}
946	      correctIndex={item.correctOption}
947	      explanation={item.explanation}

949	      onNext={onNext}
