{"file_path": "C:\\Users\\Joshu\\dev\\ai-os-projects\\lsatu-abstract\\src\\topics\\abstract\\data\\breakdownDrill.ts", "content": "// Module 3 \"Break It Down\" drill.\n//\n// Three operations, in this order, each one checked before the student moves on:\n//\n// 1. map    Find the direction words in an abstract phrase, then say which\n//           letter is the conclusion. Phrases come from Joshua's Argument\n//           Parsing and Advanced Parsing worksheets (corpus/Curriculum/Abstract).\n// 2. decode Abstract answer choice -> plain English -> which argument it\n//           describes, then the student writes a tiny argument of their own\n//           (\"Write a real-world example\" in the Level 1 worksheet). The\n//           written example is self-checked against a model, never auto-graded.\n// 3. build  Concrete argument -> the abstract description. The student\n//           assembles the answer choice from slots.\n//\n// Scenarios are original to this drill. They are not reused elsewhere in the\n// bootcamp.\n\nexport interface MapItem {\n  id: string;\n  kind: \"map\";\n  // The phrase split into tappable pieces. Exactly one piece is the\n  // direction word or phrase.\n  pieces: string[];\n  directionIndex: number;\n  conclusion: \"X\" | \"Y\";\n  directionWhy: string;\n  conclusionWhy: string;\n}\n\nexport interface Choice {\n  text: string;\n  correct: boolean;\n  why: string;\n}\n\nexport interface DecodeItem {\n  id: string;\n  kind: \"decode\";\n  answerChoice: string;\n  translations: Choice[];\n  arguments: Choice[];\n  writePrompt: string;\n  modelExample: string;\n  modelCheck: string[];\n}\n\nexport interface BuildSlot {\n  options: string[];\n  correct: number;\n  why: string;\n}\n\nexport interface BuildItem {\n  id: string;\n  kind: \"build\";\n  sentences: string[];\n  // Index of the sentence being described, or null when the item asks about\n  // the whole argument.\n  focus: number | null;\n  prompt: string;\n  // Fixed text and slots alternate: template[0], slots[0], template[1], ...\n  template: string[];\n  slots: BuildSlot[];\n}\n\nexport type BreakdownItem = MapItem | DecodeItem | BuildItem;\n\nexport const BREAKDOWN_PARTS: { kind: BreakdownItem[\"kind\"]; title: string; instruction: string }[] = [\n  {\n    kind: \"map\",\n    title: \"Which side is the conclusion?\",\n    instruction:\n      \"Tap the words that tell you which way the support runs. Then pick the letter that stands for the conclusion.\",\n  },\n  {\n    kind: \"decode\",\n    title: \"Decode it, then make it concrete\",\n    instruction:\n      \"Pick the plain-English version of the answer choice. Then pick the argument it describes. Then write a tiny argument of your own that fits it.\",\n  },\n  {\n    kind: \"build\",\n    title: \"Describe it abstractly\",\n    instruction:\n      \"Read the argument. Then build the answer choice that describes it by picking one option for each blank.\",\n  },\n];\n\nexport const BREAKDOWN_ITEMS: BreakdownItem[] = [\n  // ---------- Part 1: map ----------\n  {\n    id: \"bd_map_1\",\n    kind: \"map\",\n    pieces: [\"concludes\", \"X\", \"on the basis of\", \"Y\"],\n    directionIndex: 2,\n    conclusion: \"X\",\n    directionWhy: \"\\\"On the basis of\\\" introduces the evidence. Whatever comes after it is the basis.\",\n    conclusionWhy: \"Y is the basis, so Y is the evidence. X is what gets concluded.\",\n  },\n  {\n    id: \"bd_map_2\",\n    kind: \"map\",\n    pieces: [\"using\", \"X\", \"to support\", \"Y\"],\n    directionIndex: 2,\n    conclusion: \"Y\",\n    directionWhy: \"\\\"To support\\\" tells you X is doing the supporting.\",\n    conclusionWhy: \"X is used as support, so X is the evidence. Y is the thing being supported, so Y is the conclusion.\",\n  },\n  {\n    id: \"bd_map_3\",\n    kind: \"map\",\n    pieces: [\"infers,\", \"on the grounds that\", \"X,\", \"that\", \"Y\"],\n    directionIndex: 1,\n    conclusion: \"Y\",\n    directionWhy: \"\\\"On the grounds that\\\" introduces the grounds, which is the evidence.\",\n    conclusionWhy: \"X is the grounds. Y is what the argument infers, so Y is the conclusion.\",\n  },\n  {\n    id: \"bd_map_4\",\n    kind: \"map\",\n    pieces: [\"takes for granted that\", \"Y\", \"because\", \"X\"],\n    directionIndex: 2,\n    conclusion: \"Y\",\n    directionWhy: \"\\\"Because\\\" introduces the reason.\",\n    conclusionWhy: \"X is the reason. Y is what the argument takes for granted, so Y is the conclusion. The order of the letters doesn't matter. The direction word does.\",\n  },\n  {\n    id: \"bd_map_5\",\n    kind: \"map\",\n    pieces: [\"derives the necessity of\", \"X\", \"from\", \"the impossibility of\", \"Y\"],\n    directionIndex: 2,\n    conclusion: \"X\",\n    directionWhy: \"\\\"From\\\" points back to where the argument starts, which is the evidence.\",\n    conclusionWhy: \"The argument starts from the impossibility of Y. It ends at the necessity of X, so X is the conclusion.\",\n  },\n\n  // ---------- Part 2: decode ----------\n  {\n    id: \"bd_decode_1\",\n    kind: \"decode\",\n    answerChoice:\n      \"Rejects a position on the grounds that the proponents of the position have a vested interest in its acceptance.\",\n    translations: [\n      {\n        text: \"The argument says a view is wrong because the people pushing it would gain if others believed it.\",\n        correct: true,\n        why: \"Correct. \\\"Rejects\\\" means the argument throws the view out. \\\"Vested interest\\\" means the people behind it stand to gain.\",\n      },\n      {\n        text: \"The argument says a view is wrong because the people pushing it have no evidence.\",\n        correct: false,\n        why: \"Not that one. It doesn't accept it.\",\n      },\n    ],\n    arguments: [\n      {\n        text: \"The dentist says I need a crown. Dentists get paid for every crown they put in, so I probably don't need one.\",\n        correct: true,\n        why: \"Correct. The argument throws out the dentist's view because the dentist would profit from it.\",\n      },\n      {\n        text: \"The dentist says I need a crown. But my X-ray last month was clean, so I probably don't need one.\",\n        correct: false,\n        why: \"Not that one. This rejects the view using evidence about the tooth, not the dentist's motive.\",\n      },\n      {\n        text: \"The dentist says I need a crown, and dentists know teeth better than I do, so I should get one.\",\n        correct: false,\n        why: \"Not that one. This accepts the dentist's view. The choice describes rejecting one.\",\n      },\n    ],\n    writePrompt: \"Write a two-sentence argument that rejects a view because of who benefits from it.\",\n    modelExample:\n      \"The bottled-water company says tap water in our town is unsafe. drill_1 [role/wrong_role] key=1
  0  It is the argument's main conclusion.
      -> The main conclusion is "The new highway project will be completed ahead of schedule." It is what the author is trying to prove.
  1* It is a premise offered in support of the argument's conclusion.
      -> Correct. The claim about double shifts directly supports the conclusion that the project finished early.
  2  It is an intermediate conclusion that supports the main conclusion.
      -> An intermediate conclusion requires its own supporting evidence. The shift claim is simply assertive evidence here.
  3  It is a concession to a potential counterargument.
      -> A concession would acknowledge an opposing point of view (e.g., "Although the work was hard..."). This claim provides direct justification.

drill_2 [method/wrong_method] key=1
  0  Providing an alternative explanation for the recent outage.
      -> The IT Lead does not explain why the outage happened; they provide counter-evidence to the suggestion to switch.
  1* Pointing out that the opponent failed to take relevant information into account.
      -> Correct. The IT Lead introduces server performance data (5-year uptime, the other provider's failures) that the Manager completely ignored.
  2  Deriving a general principle from specific examples.
      -> The IT Lead is not trying to find a rule here, they are comparing performance records.
  3  Showing that the opponent's conclusion leads to an impossible result.
      -> The IT Lead does not show that switching is logically impossible; they argue it is a bad idea given the data.

drill_3 [flaw/structural_mismatch] key=1
  0  Mistakes a temporal relationship between two events for a causal relationship.
      -> The argument is not about the order of events; it is about logical requirements.
  1* Presumes, without providing justification, that a condition necessary for an outcome is also sufficient to produce that outcome.
      -> Correct. The argument mistakes necessary (must pass test) for sufficient (if she passed test, she must be allowed).
  2  Relies on an inappropriate analogy between passing a test and legal permission.
      -> There is no analogy here; it is a direct, flawed deductive inference.
  3  Attacks the person making a claim rather than the claim itself.
      -> There is no personal attack here, only a structural flaw in the reasoning.

drill_4 [one_word_trap/attractive_language] key=1
  0  It is a premise that completely proves the main conclusion that reading fiction is not a waste of time.
      -> Wrong. Premises support a conclusion; they rarely completely prove it in a formal sense. This is an overstatement.
  1* It is a premise offered to counter the claim that reading fiction is a waste of time.
      -> Correct. The claim builds empathy, which the author uses to argue against the idea that fiction is a waste of time.

drill_5 [role/wrong_role] key=1
  0  It is the main conclusion of the argument.
      -> The main conclusion is the final sentence ("we should expand, not cut, public arts funding").
  1* It is an intermediate conclusion supported by the dollar-multiplier claim and used to support the main conclusion.
      -> Correct. The dollar-multiplier evidence supports the city-economy claim, which supports the main conclusion.
  2  It is a counterconsideration the argument acknowledges before rejecting.
      -> The author is not acknowledging an opposing point here; they are advancing their own evidence chain.
  3  It is a premise that no other statement supports.
      -> The previous sentence ("every dollar generates three dollars") supports this claim, so it is not an unsupported premise.

drill_6 [role/wrong_role] key=0
  0* It is background information that sets context but does not support the main conclusion.
      -> Correct. "Although" signals a concession or background framing. The flooding worry is not used as evidence.
  1  It is the main conclusion of the argument.
      -> The conclusion is about the dam's effect on flood damage.
  2  It is a premise offered in direct support of the conclusion.
      -> The claim is bracketed by "although" and does not feed into the dam's effectiveness argument.
  3  It is a counterexample to the claim that floods come from river overflow.
      -> It does not contradict the river-overflow claim; it just notes homeowner concern.

drill_7 [role/wrong_role] key=0
  0* It is a premise offered in support of the claim that news outlets have a financial incentive.
      -> Correct. "Polls drive viewership" plus "viewership drives advertising revenue" supports the financial-incentive claim, which supports the main conclusion.
  1  It is the main conclusion of the argument.
      -> The main conclusion is about the economic explanation for poll prominence.
  2  It is a counterexample to the claim that polling is unreliable.
      -> The claim does not bear on polling reliability.
  3  It is a generalization that the argument later rejects.
      -> The argument uses, not rejects, this claim.

drill_8 [role/wrong_role] key=1
  0  It is the main conclusion of the argument.
      -> The conclusion is that minimum wage increases do not necessarily reduce employment.
  1* It is a premise offered to counter the opposing economists' view.
      -> Correct. The buying-power chain is the author's counter-evidence against the reduce-employment claim.
  2  It is a claim the author concedes for the sake of argument before rejecting.
      -> The author affirms, not concedes, this claim.
  3  It is a hypothesis the argument tries to undermine.
      -> The argument uses this claim as support, not as a target.

drill_9 [method/wrong_method] key=0
  0* Providing an alternative explanation for the observed effect.
      -> Correct. The author replaces the camera explanation with the infrastructure-repair explanation.
  1  Attacking the officials by impugning their motives.
      -> No motive attack appears in the argument.
  2  Showing that the officials' conclusion leads to an impossible result.
      -> The author does not derive an absurd consequence; they offer a competing cause.
  3  Appealing to the testimony of an expert.
      -> No expert is cited.

drill_10 [method/wrong_method] key=1
  0  Inferring a causal relationship from a correlation.
      -> No correlation is the basis here; the author rules out two of three options.
  1* Establishing one cause by excluding alternative causes.
      -> Correct. Method 2 from the 17 Methods source: validate one cause by eliminating alternatives.
  2  Appealing to a definition of bee health.
      -> No definitions are invoked.
  3  Using a counterexample to attack a generalization.
      -> There is no generalization being attacked.

drill_11 [method/wrong_method] key=1
  0  Calling into question a premise on which Hill's argument depends.
      -> Watkins does not dispute that breakfast-eaters weigh less; she challenges the inference, not the data.
  1* Reinterpreting Hill's evidence as also compatible with an alternative explanation.
      -> Correct. Method 3 from the 17 Methods source. Watkins accepts the data but offers exercise and sleep as alternative causes.
  2  Pointing out an inappropriate analogy in Hill's reasoning.
      -> Hill makes no analogy.
  3  Appealing to the testimony of a nutrition expert.
      -> Watkins cites no expert.

drill_12 [method/wrong_method] key=0
  0* Pointing out that the pundits failed to take relevant information into account.
      -> Correct. Method 8 from the 17 Methods source. The author introduces the cost-of-compliance asymmetry that the pundits ignored.
  1  Drawing a general principle from specific cases.
      -> No generalization is being drawn; the author critiques a specific claim.
  2  Citing a counterexample to a broad generalization.
      -> The pundits are not making a generalization here, they are making a prediction.
  3  Inferring a causal relationship from a correlation.
      -> No correlation is offered as the basis.

drill_13 [method/wrong_method] key=0
  0* Showing that Vega's proposal would have undesirable consequences.
      -> Correct. Method 7 from the 17 Methods source. Lin argues the policy itself, if enacted, leads to bad outcomes.
  1  Attacking Vega's premise that children's advertising is manipulative.
      -> Lin does not dispute that some advertising is manipulative.
  2  Pointing out an inappropriate analogy in Vega's argument.
      -> Vega offers no analogy.
  3  Calling into question the sufficiency of Vega's evidence.
      -> Lin's objection is about the policy's consequences, not the evidence's strength.

drill_14 [flaw/structural_mismatch] key=0
  0* Confuses what people report with what is actually the case.
      -> Correct. People's self-reports about feeling efficient do not directly establish that they are more efficient.
  1  Treats a necessary condition as if it were a sufficient one.
      -> No necessary/sufficient confusion appears here.
  2  Mistakes a temporal sequence for a causal relationship.
      -> No temporal sequence is invoked.
  3  Generalizes from a sample that is unrepresentative.
      -> The flaw is the gap between feeling and being efficient, not sample size.

drill_15 [flaw/structural_mismatch] key=0
  0* Generalizes from a single case to a broad conclusion.
      -> Correct. One person's experience does not establish that the plan works in general.
  1  Confuses correlation with causation.
      -> While there is a temporal sequence, the deeper flaw is the sample size: one person.
  2  Rejects a hypothesis without offering an alternative.
      -> The argument accepts, not rejects, a hypothesis.
  3  Attacks the person rather than the claim.
      -> No personal attack appears.

drill_16 [flaw/structural_mismatch] key=0
  0* Confuses the distribution of a feature within a group with the cause of becoming a member of that group.
      -> Correct. The fact that pros attended camps does not establish that camps cause people to become pros.
  1  Generalizes from a sample that is too small to support the conclusion.
      -> Sample size is not the central problem; the issue is direction of inference.
  2  Attacks the credibility of music professionals rather than their claims.
      -> There is no attack on credibility.
  3  Mistakes a sufficient condition for a necessary one.
      -> No necessary/sufficient flip occurs here.

drill_17 [flaw/structural_mismatch] key=0
  0* Treats note-taking medium as the only factor relevant to laptop use in class.
      -> Correct. The conclusion is about laptop use generally, but the evidence only addresses one purpose for the laptop.
  1  Confuses correlation with causation.
      -> Causation is plausibly established by controlled studies; the gap is the conclusion's overreach.
  2  Generalizes from a single case.
      -> The argument cites studies, not a single case.
  3  Mistakes the absence of evidence for the evidence of absence.
      -> Studies provide evidence; this is not an absence-of-evidence move.

drill_18 [one_word_trap/attractive_language] key=0
  0* The argument treats a necessary condition as if it were sufficient.
      -> Correct. "Always guarantees" is the trap word. The evidence shows three rounds were necessary; the conclusion claims they are sufficient.
  1  The argument treats a sufficient condition as if it were necessary.
      -> The reverse of the actual flip. The evidence is about a feature shared by successful launches, not about what is enough to cause success.

drill_19 [one_word_trap/attractive_language] key=0
  0* Strengthens the researcher's tentative finding into a universal prescription.
      -> Correct. "Suggests" and "may" become "proves" and "everyone should". The trap is the strength escalation.
  1  Rejects the researcher's finding without offering counter-evidence.
      -> The advocate accepts, not rejects, the finding.

drill_20 [one_word_trap/attractive_language] key=1
  0  It conclusively proves that the camera did not cause the reduction.
      -> Wrong. "Conclusively proves" is the trap word. The author shows an alternative is consistent, not that the camera explanation is impossible.
  1* It offers an alternative explanation that is consistent with the same observed effect.
      -> Correct. The author opens room for another cause without ruling out the original.

  letter-spacing: 0;

@media (min-width: 768px) {
  .topic-abstract .lesson-cased p {
    font-size: 1.125rem;
  }
}

/* Demote any inline text that is shouting in the legacy lesson markup:
 * any element sized above text-base AND wearing uppercase or italic
 * loses those treatments. text-xs and text-[10px] labels keep theirs. */
.topic-abstract .lesson-cased .text-lg,
.topic-abstract .lesson-cased .text-xl,
.topic-abstract .lesson-cased .text-2xl,
.topic-abstract .lesson-cased .text-3xl,
.topic-abstract .lesson-cased .text-4xl,
.topic-abstract .lesson-cased .text-5xl,
.topic-abstract .lesson-cased .text-6xl {
  text-transform: none;
  font-style: normal;
}

/* Body paragraphs in legacy LessonReader markup are typically tagged
 * font-bold; demote to normal weight here. font-black remains on
 * actual headers (sized above text-base) where the cn() chain still
 * applies black. */
.topic-abstract .lesson-cased p.font-bold,
.topic-abstract .lesson-cased p .font-bold {
  font-weight: 400;
}

/* Strong + em inside paragraphs keep their semantics in cased form. */
.topic-abstract .lesson-cased p strong {
  font-weight: 700;
}

.topic-abstract .lesson-cased p em {
  font-style: italic;
  font-weight: inherit;
}

.topic-abstract .lesson-cased ul,
.topic-abstract .lesson-cased ol {
  font-size: 1rem;
  line-height: 1.7;
  margin-bottom: 1.25rem;
  padding-left: 1.5rem;
}

.topic-abstract .lesson-cased li {
  margin-bottom: 0.5rem;
  font-weight: 400;
}

className=\"text-lg md:text-xl font-bold leading-snug\">{children}</div>\n    </div>\n  );\n}\n\nfunction Label({ children }: { children: React.ReactNode }) {\n  return (\n    <span className=\"block text-[11px] font-black uppercase tracking-[0.18em] text-brutal-ink-soft mb-2\">\n      {children}\n    </span>\n  );\n}\n\nfunction AnswerChoice({ children }: { children: React.ReactNode }) {\n  return (\n    <div className=\"bg-white border-4 border-brutal-ink p-5 md:p-6 my-4 brutal-shadow-sm\">\n      <Label>Answer choice</Label>\n      <div className=\"text-lg md:text-xl font-semibold leading-snug\">{children}</div>\n    </div>\n  );\n}\n\nfunction Verdict({ ok, children }: { ok: boolean; children: React.ReactNode }) {\n  return (\n    <div\n      className={cn(\n        \"mt-3 border-2 border-brutal-ink p-4 flex gap-3 items-start\",\n        ok ? \"bg-brutal-green/40\" : \"bg-brutal-red/15\",\n      )}\n      role=\"status\"\n    >\n      <span\n        className={cn(\n          \"shrink-0 inline-flex items-center gap-1 border-2 border-brutal-ink px-2 py-0.5 text-[11px] font-black uppercase tracking-wider\",\n          ok ? \"bg-brutal-green\" : \"bg-brutal-red text-brutal-paper\",\n        )}\n      >\n        {ok ? <Check className=\"w-3.5 h-3.5\" aria-hidden /> : <X className=\"w-3.5 h-3.5\" aria-hidden />}\n        {ok ? \"Correct\" : \"Not quite\"}\n      </span>\n      <span className=\"text-base leading-relaxed\">{children}</span>\n    </div>\n  );\n}\n\nfunction RightWrongCard({ ok, title, children }: { ok: boolean; title: string; children: React.ReactNode }) {\n  return (\n    <div\n      className={cn(\n        \"border-2 border-brutal-ink p-4 flex gap-3 items-start\",\n        ok ? \"bg-brutal-green/30\" : \"bg-brutal-red/10\",\n      )}\n    >\n      <span\n        className={cn(\n          \"shrink-0 inline-flex items-center gap-1 border-2 border-brutal-ink px-2 py-0.5 text-[11px] font-black uppercase tracking-wider\",\n          ok ? \"bg-brutal-green\" : \"bg-brutal-red text-brutal-paper\",\n        )}\n      >\n        {ok ? <Check className=\"w-3.5 h-3.5\" aria-hidden /> : <X className=\"w-3.5 h-3.5\" aria-hidden />}\n        {ok ? \"Right\" : \"Wrong\"}\n      </span>\n      <div className=\"text-base leading-relaxed\">\n        <span className=\"font-bold\">{title}</span> {children}\n      </div>\n    </div>\n  );\n}\n\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------- Module 1\n\nconst POOL_SENTENCES = [\n  \"The town should keep the public pool open through September.\",\n  \"More families now stay in town into early fall.\",\n  \"After all, the school year now starts two weeks later than it used to.\",\n];\n\nfunction WhichSentence() {\n  const [picked, setPicked] = useState<number | null>(null);\n  const correct = 1;\n  return (\n    <div className=\"my-6\">\n      <Label>The argument</Label>\n      <ol className=\"list-none p-0 m-0 space-y-2\">\n        {POOL_SENTENCES.map((s, i) => {\n          const isPicked = picked === i;\n          return (\n            <li key={i} className=\"m-0\">\n              <button\n                type=\"button\"\n                onClick={() => setPicked(i)}\n                aria-pressed={isPicked}\n                className={cn(\n                  \"w-full text-left border-2 border-brutal-ink p-3 md:p-4 text-base leading-relaxed transition-colors\",\n                  picked === null && \"bg-white hover:bg-brutal-yellow/40\",\n                  picked !== null && i === correct && \"bg-brutal-green/50\",\n                  picked !== null && isPicked && i !== correct && \"bg-brutal-red/20\",\n                  picked !== null && !isPicked && i !== correct && \"bg-white\",\n                )}\n              >\n                <span className=\"font-black mr-2\">({i + 1})</span>\n                {s}\n              </button>\n            </li>\n          );\n        })}\n      </ol>\n      <AnswerChoice>It is supported by another statement, and it supports the main conclusion.</AnswerChoice>\n      <p className=\"font-semibold\">Click the sentence this answer choice is describing.</p>\n      {picked !== null &&\n        (picked === correct ? (\n          <Verdict ok>\n            Sentence 2. Sentence 3 supports it (later school year, so families stay), and it supports sentence 1\n            (families are here, so keep the pool open).\n          </Verdict>\n        ) : (\n          <Verdict ok={false}>\n            {picked === 0\n              ? \"Sentence 1 is the main conclusion. The choice describes something that supports the main conclusion.\"\n              : \"Sentence 3 supports another statement, but nothing supports it. The choice describes a sentence that has support of its own.\"}{\" \"}\n            Try again.\n          </Verdict>\n        ))}\n      {picked === correct && (\n        <div className=\"grid md:grid-cols-2 gap-4 mt-6\">\n          <div className=\"bg-white border-2 border-brutal-ink p-4\">\n            <Label>Concrete</Label>\n            <p className=\"m-0\">More families now stay in town into early fall.</p>\n          </div>\n          <div className=\"bg-brutal-yellow/50 border-2 border-brutal-ink p-4\">\n            <Label>Abstract</Label>\n            <p className=\"m-0\">A claim that is supported by another statement and supports the main conclusion.</p>\n          </div>\n        </div>\n      )}\n    </div>\n  );\n}\n\nexport function WhyHardNote() {\n  return (\n    <div>\n      <Panel>\n        An abstract answer choice describes what part of an argument does, without naming what the argument is\n        about.\n      </Panel>\n      <p>\n        Role, Method and Flaw questions are full of them. The stimulus talks about a pool, a library or a\n        dentist. The answer choices never do. They talk about \"a premise,\" \"a claim,\" \"the conclusion.\"\n      </p>\n\n      <h3>See one</h3>\n      <WhichSentence />\n      <p>\n        Same sentence, two descriptions. The abstract one strips out the pool and the families and keeps only the\n        job the sentence does in the argument.\n      </p>\n\n      <h3>Why they feel hard</h3>\n      <p>\n        <strong>1. Everyday words, strict meanings.</strong> On the LSAT, \"explanation\" means cause and\n        \"presuppose\" means require. Read them the everyday way and you misread the choice.\n      </p>\n      <p>\n        <strong>2. Nothing to hold onto.</strong> There is no pool in the answer choice. You have to supply the\n        content yourself by going back to the stimulus.\n      </p>\n      <p>\n        <strong>3. One word can sink it.</strong> A choice can describe the argument perfectly except for one\n        word, like \"proves\" where the author only \"suggests.\" That one word makes it wrong.\n      </p>\n\n      <h3>How this bootcamp fixes it</h3>\n      <p>\n        First the words (Module 2). Then a routine for breaking any abstract choice into plain English (Module 3)\n        and matching every piece to the stimulus (Module 4). Then methods and traps (Modules 5 and 6). Module 7\n        puts it all together.\n      </p>\n      <Panel tone=\"yellow\">An abstract choice is a description. Your job is to find what it describes.</Panel>\n    </div>\n  );\n}\n\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------- Module 2\n\nconst STATEMENT_WORDS: { term: string; meaning: string; note?: string }[] = [\n  { term: \"Premise\", meaning: \"A reason the author gives to support a conclusion.\" },\n  { term: \"Main conclusion\", meaning: \"The final point the author wants you to accept.\" },\n  {\n    term: \"Intermediate conclusion\",\n    meaning: \"A claim that gets support and then gives support to the main conclusion.\",\n    note: \"Also called a supported premise.\",\n  },\n  { term: \"Fact\", meaning: \"A statement about the world, taken as true.\", note: \"\\\"Water boils at 100 degrees Celsius at sea level.\\\"\" },\n  { term: \"Decision\", meaning: \"A choice someone made or will make.\", note: \"\\\"I'm taking the train instead of the bus.\\\"\" },\n  {\n    term: \"Principle (general principle)\",\n    meaning: \"A rule about what should or shouldn't be done that covers many cases.\",\n    note: \"\\\"People who are tired every morning should go to bed earlier\\\" is a principle. \\\"John is tired every morning, so he should go to bed earlier\\\" is not. It's about one person.\",\n  },\n  {\n    term: \"Generalization\",\n    meaning: \"A broad statement about what is.\",\n    note: \"\\\"All swans are white.\\\" A principle says what should be. A generalization says what is.\",\n  },\n  {\n    term: \"Hypothesis\",\n    meaning: \"A possible explanation: a proposed cause.\",\n    note: \"Not just any claim. A hypothesis is a guess about why something happened.\",\n  },\n];\n\nconst ACTION_WORDS: { term: string; meaning: string; note?: string }[] = [\n  { term: \"Illustrate\", meaning: \"Give an example.\", note: \"\\\"It illustrates a principle\\\" means it's an example of the principle.\" },\n  {\n    term: \"Presuppose\",\n    meaning: \"Require.\",\n    note: \"If the argument presupposes something, the argument falls apart without it.\",\n  },\n  { term: \"Explain\", meaning: \"Give the cause.\", note: \"Not add detail. An explanation is a cause.\" },\n  { term: \"Undermine\", meaning: \"Weaken. Push against.\" },\n  {\n    term: \"Defend\",\n    meaning: \"Argue that a position is right.\",\n    note: \"Saying why someone holds a view is not defending it.\",\n  },\n];\n\nfunction WordGrid({ words, accent }: { words: typeof STATEMENT_WORDS; accent: \"cyan\" | \"pink\" }) {\n  return (\n    <div className=\"grid md:grid-cols-2 gap-4 my-6\">\n      {words.map((w) => (\n        <div key={w.term} className=\"bg-white border-2 border-brutal-ink p-4 brutal-shadow-sm\">\n          <div\n            className={cn(\n              \"inline-block border-2 border-brutal-ink px-2 py-0.5 mb-2 text-sm font-black\",\n              accent === \"cyan\" ? \"bg-brutal-cyan\" : \"bg-brutal-pink\",\n            )}\n          >\n            {w.term}\n          </div>\n          <p className=\"m-0 font-semibold\">{w.meaning}</p>\n          {w.note && <p className=\"m-0 mt-2 text-brutal-ink-soft\">{w.note}</p>}\n        </div>\n      ))}\n    </div>\n  );\n}\n\nexport function WordsNote() {\n  return (\n    <div>\n      <Panel>\n        These words look ordinary. On the LSAT each one has a strict meaning, and abstract answer choices are built\n        out of them.\n      </Panel>\n      <p>\n        There are thirteen. The first eight name what a statement can be. The last five name what an argument can\n        do.\n      </p>\n      <h3>What a statement can be</h3>\n      <WordGrid words={STATEMENT_WORDS} accent=\"cyan\" />\n      <h3>What an argument can do</h3>\n      <WordGrid words={ACTION_WORDS} accent=\"pink\" />\n      <p>\n        When one of these shows up in an answer choice, swap in the strict meaning before you go back to the\n        stimulus. That swap is Step 2 of the routine in Module 3.\n      </p>\n    </div>\n  );\n}\n\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------- Module 3\n\nconst DEMO_CHOICE =\n  \"It is a hypothesis that the argument attempts to undermine by calling into question the sufficiency of the evidence.\";\nconst DEMO_KEYWORDS = new Set([\"hypothesis\", \"undermine\", \"sufficiency\", \"evidence\"]);\nconst DEMO_NEUTRAL = new Set([\"calling\", \"into\", \"question\", \"attempts\"]);\n\nconst DEMO_TRANSLATIONS = [\n  { word: \"hypothesis\", meaning: \"a possible explanation (a proposed cause)\" },\n  { word: \"undermine\", meaning: \"weaken, push against\" },\n  { word: \"sufficiency of the evidence\", meaning: \"whether the evidence is enough\" },\n];\n\nconst DEMO_REQUIREMENTS = [\n  \"A possible explanation: someone proposes a cause.\",\n  \"The argument pushes against that explanation.\",\n  \"The push is that the evidence for it is too thin.\",\n];\n\nconst DEMO_STIMULI: { name: string; text: string; present: boolean[]; why: string[] }[] = [\n  {\n    name: \"Stimulus A\",\n    text: \"Some researchers blame a new pesticide for the drop in the town's songbirds. But they tested soil from only two farms, both right next to the highway. Two samples can't carry that conclusion.\",\n    present: [true, true, true],\n    why: [\n      \"The researchers propose a cause: the pesticide.\",\n      \"\\\"But\\\" starts the push against it.\",\n      \"Two samples from two farms is the evidence being called too thin.\",\n    ],\n  },\n  {\n    name: \"Stimulus B\",\n    text: \"Some researchers blame a new pesticide for the drop in the town's songbirds. But songbirds also vanished from the next town over, which has never used the pesticide.\",\n    present: [true, true, false],\n    why: [\n      \"The researchers propose a cause: the pesticide.\",\n      \"\\\"But\\\" starts the push against it.\",\n      \"The author never says the researchers' evidence is too thin. The push is new counter-evidence, so this piece is missing.\",\n    ],\n  },\n];\n\nfunction tokenize(text: string): string[] {\n  return text.split(/(\\s+)/).filter((t) => t.length > 0);\n}\n\nfunction clean(word: string): string {\n  return word.replace(/[.,;:?!\"]/g, \"\").toLowerCase();\n}\n\nfunction BreakdownDemo() {\n  const tokens = useMemo(() => tokenize(DEMO_CHOICE), []);\n  const [picked, setPicked] = useState<Set<number>>(new Set());\n  const [checked, setChecked] = useState(false);\n  const [translated, setTranslated] = useState<Set<number>>(new Set());\n  const [stimIndex, setStimIndex] = useState(0);\n  const [marks, setMarks] = useState<Record<string, boolean>>({});\n\n  const pickedWords = Array.from(picked).map((i) => clean(tokens[i]));\n  const hits = Array.from(DEMO_KEYWORDS).filter((k) => pickedWords.includes(k));\n  const filler = pickedWords.filter((w) => !DEMO_KEYWORDS.has(w) && !DEMO_NEUTRAL.has(w));\n  const allTranslated = translated.size === DEMO_TRANSLATIONS.length;\n  const stim = DEMO_STIMULI[stimIndex];\n  const stimDone = stim.present.every((_, r) => marks[`${stimIndex}-${r}`] !== undefined);\n  const stimSurvives = stim.present.every((p) => p);\n\n  return (\n    <div className=\"border-4 border-brutal-ink bg-brutal-paper p-5 md:p-7 my-8 brutal-shadow\">\n      <Label>Try it</Label>\n\n      {/* Step 1 */}\n      <p className=\"mb-3\">\n        <strong>Step 1. Click the words that carry the meaning.</strong> Leave the filler alone.\n      </p>\n      <div className=\"bg-white border-2 border-brutal-ink p-4 text-lg md:text-xl leading-loose\">\n        {tokens.map((t, i) => {\n          if (/^\\s+$/.test(t)) return <span key={i}>{t}</span>;\n          const w = clean(t);\n          const isPicked = picked.has(i);\n          const isKey = DEMO_KEYWORDS.has(w);\n          return (\n            <button\n              key={i}\n              type=\"button\"\n              disabled={checked}\n              aria-pressed={isPicked}\n              onClick={() =>\n                setPicked((prev) => {\n                  const next = new Set(prev);\n                  if (next.has(i)) next.delete(i);\n                  else next.add(i);\n                  return next;\n                })\n              }\n              className={cn(\n                \"px-0.5 border-b-2 border-transparent transition-colors font-semibold\",\n                !checked && isPicked && \"bg-brutal-yellow border-brutal-ink\",\n                !checked && !isPicked && \"hover:bg-brutal-yellow/40\",\n                checked && isKey && \"bg-brutal-green/60 border-brutal-ink\",\n                checked && isPicked && !isKey && !DEMO_NEUTRAL.has(w) && \"bg-brutal-red/30 line-through\",\n              )}\n            >\n              {t}\n            </button>\n          );\n        })}\n      </div>\n      {!checked ? (\n        <button\n          type=\"button\"\n          disabled={picked.size === 0}\n          onClick={() => setChecked(true)}\n          className=\"brutal-btn bg-brutal-yellow mt-4 disabled:opacity-50\"\n        >\n          Check my words\n        </button>\n      ) : (\n        <Verdict ok={hits.length === DEMO_KEYWORDS.size && filler.length === 0}>\n          {hits.length === DEMO_KEYWORDS.size\n            ? \"You found all four: hypothesis, undermine, sufficiency, evidence.\"\n            : `You found ${hits.length} of 4. The keywords are hypothesis, undermine, sufficiency and evidence (highlighted green).`}{\" \"}\n          {filler.length > 0\n            ? `\"${filler.join('\", \"')}\" ${filler.length === 1 ? \"is\" : \"are\"} filler. Words like it, is, the and argument appear in almost every choice, so they can't tell choices apart.`\n            : \"\"}\n        </Verdict>\n      )}\n\n      {/* Step 2 */}\n      {checked && (\n        <div className=\"mt-8\">\n          <p className=\"mb-3\">\n            <strong>Step 2. Translate each keyword.</strong> Click each one to swap in its strict meaning.\n          </p>\n          <div className=\"grid md:grid-cols-3 gap-3\">\n            {DEMO_TRANSLATIONS.map((t, i) => {\n              const open = translated.has(i);\n              return (\n                <button\n                  key={t.word}\n                  type=\"button\"\n                  aria-expanded={open}\n                  onClick={() => setTranslated((prev) => new Set(prev).add(i))}\n                  className={cn(\n                    \"text-left border-2 border-brutal-ink p-4 transition-colors min-h-[96px]\",\n                    open ? \"bg-brutal-cyan/40\" : \"bg-white hover:bg-brutal-cyan/20\",\n                  )}\n                >\n                  <span className=\"block font-black\">{t.word}</span>\n                  <span className=\"block mt-1 text-base\">{open ? `= ${t.meaning}` : \"Click to translate\"}</span>\n                </button>\n              );\n            })}\n          </div>\n        </div>\n      )}\n\n      {/* Step 3 */}\n      {allTranslated && (\n        <div className=\"mt-8\">\n          <p className=\"mb-3\">\n            <strong>Step 3. Say it as one plain sentence.</strong>\n          </p>\n          <div className=\"bg-brutal-yellow border-2 border-brutal-ink p-4 text-lg font-semibold\">\n            The argument tries to weaken a possible explanation by saying the evidence for it isn't enough.\n          </div>\n        </div>\n      )}\n\n      {/* Step 4 */}\n      {allTranslated && (\n        <div className=\"mt-8\">\n          <p className=\"mb-3\">\n            <strong>Step 4. List what the stimulus must contain, then check it.</strong> One item per piece. For\n            each item, decide whether it is there.\n          </p>\n          <div className=\"flex gap-2 mb-3\" role=\"tablist\" aria-label=\"Stimuli to check\">\n            {DEMO_STIMULI.map((s, i) => (\n              <button\n                key={s.name}\n                type=\"button\"\n                role=\"tab\"\n                aria-selected={stimIndex === i}\n                onClick={() => setStimIndex(i)}\n                className={cn(\n                  \"border-2 border-brutal-ink px-4 py-2 text-sm font-black\",\n                  stimIndex === i ? \"bg-brutal-ink text-brutal-paper\" : \"bg-white\",\n                )}\n              >\n                {s.name}\n              </button>\n            ))}\n          </div>\n          <div className=\"bg-white border-2 border-brutal-ink p-4 mb-4 text-base leading-relaxed\">{stim.text}</div>\n          <ol className=\"list-none p-0 m-0 space-y-3\">\n            {DEMO_REQUIREMENTS.map((req, r) => {\n              const key = `${stimIndex}-${r}`;\n              const mark = marks[key];\n              const answered = mark !== undefined;\n              const right = answered && mark === stim.present[r];\n              return (\n                <li key={key} className=\"m-0 border-2 border-brutal-ink bg-white p-3\">\n                  <div className=\"flex flex-col md:flex-row md:items-center gap-3\">\n                    <span className=\"flex-1 font-semibold\">\n                      {r + 1}. {req}\n                    </span>\n                    <div className=\"flex gap-2 shrink-0\">\n                      {[true, false].map((val) => (\n                        <button\n                          key={String(val)}\n                          type=\"button\"\n                          disabled={answered}\n                          aria-pressed={mark === val}\n                          onClick={() => setMarks((m) => ({ ...m, [key]: val }))}\n                          className={cn(\n                            \"border-2 border-brutal-ink px-3 py-1.5 text-sm font-black min-w-[88px]\",\n                            mark === val ? \"bg-brutal-ink text-brutal-paper\" : \"bg-white hover:bg-brutal-yellow/40\",\n                          )}\n                        >\n                          {val ? \"It's there\" : \"Missing\"}\n                        </button>\n                      ))}\n                    </div>\n                  </div>\n                  {answered && (\n                    <Verdict ok={right}>\n                      {stim.present[r] ? \"It's there. \" : \"It's missing. \"}\n                      {stim.why[r]}\n                    </Verdict>\n                  )}\n                </li>\n              );\n            })}\n          </ol>\n          {stimDone && (\n            <div\n              className={cn(\n                \"mt-4 border-4 border-brutal-ink p-4 text-lg font-black\",\n                stimSurvives ? \"bg-brutal-green\" : \"bg-brutal-red text-brutal-paper\",\n              )}\n            >\n              {stimSurvives\n                ? \"Every piece is there. For this stimulus, the answer choice stays in.\"\n                : \"One piece is missing. For this stimulus, the answer choice is out.\"}\n            </div>\n          )}\n          {stimDone && stimIndex === 0 && (\n            <button type=\"button\" onClick={() => setStimIndex(1)} className=\"brutal-btn bg-brutal-yellow mt-4\">\n              Now check Stimulus B\n            </button>\n          )}\n        </div>\n      )}\n    </div>\n  );\n}\n\nconst DIRECTION_EXAMPLES = [\n  { phrase: \"infers that X on the grounds that Y\", conclusion: \"X\", evidence: \"Y\", word: \"on the grounds that\" },\n  { phrase: \"achieves X by doing Y\", conclusion: \"X (the goal)\", evidence: \"Y (the method)\", word: \"by\" },\n  { phrase: \"treats X as evidence for Y\", conclusion: \"Y\", evidence: \"X\", word: \"evidence for\" },\n];\n\nexport function BreakItDownNote() {\n  return (\n    <div>\n      <Panel>\n        Breaking down an abstract answer choice means turning it into one plain sentence, then listing what the\n        stimulus must contain for the choice to be right.\n      </Panel>\n      <p>Here's an answer choice from a Role question:</p>\n      <AnswerChoice>{DEMO_CHOICE}</AnswerChoice>\n      <p>That's a lot of words. The idea underneath is simple, and four steps get you to it.</p>\n\n      <h3>The four steps</h3>\n      <p>\n        <strong>Step 1. Pull out the keywords.</strong> Keep the words that carry meaning: nouns, verbs and\n        qualifiers. Skip filler like \"it is\" and \"the argument.\"\n      </p>\n      <p>\n        <strong>Step 2. Translate each keyword.</strong> Swap in the strict LSAT meaning from Module 2.\n      </p>\n      <p>\n        <strong>Step 3. Say it as one plain sentence.</strong>\n      </p>\n      <p>\n        <strong>Step 4. List what the stimulus must contain.</strong> One item for each piece. If the stimulus is\n        missing any one of them, the choice is wrong.\n      </p>\n\n      <BreakdownDemo />\n\n      <h3>Which part is the conclusion?</h3>\n      <p>\n        Some abstract choices describe the whole argument with placeholders, like \"concludes X on the basis of Y.\"\n        Before you can match one, you need to know which letter is the conclusion and which is the evidence. The\n        direction words tell you.\n      </p>\n      <div className=\"overflow-x-auto my-6\">\n        <table className=\"w-full border-2 border-brutal-ink bg-white text-left text-base\">\n          <thead className=\"bg-brutal-ink text-brutal-paper\">\n            <tr>\n              <th className=\"p-3\">Abstract phrase</th>\n              <th className=\"p-3\">Direction word</th>\n              <th className=\"p-3\">Conclusion</th>\n              <th className=\"p-3\">Evidence</th>\n            </tr>\n          </thead>\n          <tbody>\n            {DIRECTION_EXAMPLES.map((row) => (\n              <tr key={row.phrase} className=\"border-t-2 border-brutal-ink\">\n                <td className=\"p-3 font-semibold\">{row.phrase}</td>\n                <td className=\"p-3\">\n                  <span className=\"bg-brutal-yellow px-1\">{row.word}</span>\n                </td>\n                <td className=\"p-3\">{row.conclusion}</td>\n                <td className=\"p-3\">{row.evidence}</td>\n              </tr>\n            ))}\n          </tbody>\n        </table>\n      </div>\n      <p>\n        Words like <strong>from</strong>, <strong>by</strong>, <strong>because</strong> and{\" \"}\n        <strong>on the basis of</strong> point back to the evidence. The order of the letters doesn't matter. The\n        direction word does.\n      </p>\n\n      <h3>Now go the other way</h3>\n      <p>\n        The best test of whether you understand an abstract choice is to make it concrete. Write a two-sentence\n        argument that fits it. If you can't, you don't understand the choice yet.\n      </p>\n      <AnswerChoice>\n        Presumes, without providing justification, that a condition necessary for an outcome is also sufficient to\n        produce that outcome.\n      </AnswerChoice>\n      <div className=\"grid md:grid-cols-2 gap-4 my-4\">\n        <div className=\"bg-brutal-yellow/50 border-2 border-brutal-ink p-4\">\n          <Label>Plain English</Label>\n          <p className=\"m-0\">It treats a requirement as if it were a guarantee.</p>\n        </div>\n        <div className=\"bg-white border-2 border-brutal-ink p-4\">\n          <Label>Concrete</Label>\n          <p className=\"m-0\">To win the lottery, you need a ticket. I bought a ticket, so I'm going to win.</p>\n        </div>\n      </div>\n      <p>\n        The drill for this module has you do both directions: abstract choices into concrete arguments, and\n        concrete arguments into abstract choices.\n      </p>\n\n      <h3>What breaking it down is not</h3>\n      <div className=\"space-y-3 my-6\">\n        <RightWrongCard ok={false} title=\"Summarizing the stimulus.\">\n          You're translating the answer choice, not the argument.\n        </RightWrongCard>\n        <RightWrongCard ok={false} title=\"Asking whether the choice sounds related.\">\n          Related isn't enough. Every piece has to be there.\n        </RightWrongCard>\n        <RightWrongCard ok title=\"Asking whether you can point to every piece in the stimulus.\">\n          That's the whole test.\n        </RightWrongCard>\n      </div>\n    </div>\n  );\n}\n\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------- Module 4\n\nconst MATCH_STIMULUS =\n  \"Store managers claim the self-checkout machines made lines longer. But average wait times are the same as they were last year. So the managers are wrong.\";\n\nconst MATCH_CHOICES: { text: string; pieces: { piece: string; quote: string | null; note: string }[] }[] = [\n  {\n    text: \"It cites evidence to reject a claim about an effect.\",\n    pieces: [\n      { piece: \"cites evidence\", quote: \"average wait times are the same as they were last year\", note: \"The evidence.\" },\n      { piece: \"to reject\", quote: \"So the managers are wrong\", note: \"The author rejects something.\" },\n      { piece: \"a claim about an effect\", quote: \"the self-checkout machines made lines longer\", note: \"Machines (cause) made lines longer (effect).\" },\n    ],\n  },\n  {\n    text: \"It cites evidence to show that a claim about an effect is exaggerated.\",\n    pieces: [\n      { piece: \"cites evidence\", quote: \"average wait times are the same as they were last year\", note: \"The evidence.\" },\n      { piece: \"a claim about an effect\", quote: \"the self-checkout machines made lines longer\", note: \"Same claim as before.\" },\n      { piece: \"is exaggerated\", quote: null, note: \"Nothing to point to. The author says the managers are wrong, not that they overstated it.\" },\n    ],\n  },\n];\n\nexport function MatchingNote() {\n  const [choice, setChoice] = useState(0);\n  const [shown, setShown] = useState<Set<string>>(new Set());\n  const c = MATCH_CHOICES[choice];\n  return (\n    <div>\n      <Panel>\n        Breaking a choice down gives you a list. Matching means pointing to each item on that list in the stimulus.\n        If you can't point to one, the choice is wrong.\n      </Panel>\n\n      <h3>How to point</h3>\n      <p>Each kind of word gets its own check.</p>\n      <p>\n        <strong>Nouns: is the thing there?</strong> If the choice says \"a principle,\" find the principle. If it says\n        \"an objection,\" find the objection.\n      </p>\n      <p>\n        <strong>Verbs: does the author actually do it?</strong> \"Rejects,\" \"supports,\" \"concludes.\" Find the words\n        where the author does exactly that.\n      </p>\n      <p>\n        <strong>Qualifiers: is the strength right?</strong> \"Proves\" is not \"suggests.\" \"All\" is not \"some.\"\n        \"False\" is not \"not shown.\"\n      </p>\n\n      <h3>See it</h3>\n      <div className=\"bg-white border-2 border-brutal-ink p-4 my-4 text-base leading-relaxed\">\n        <Label>Stimulus</Label>\n        {MATCH_STIMULUS}\n      </div>\n      <div className=\"flex flex-wrap gap-2 mb-3\" role=\"tablist\" aria-label=\"Answer choices\">\n        {MATCH_CHOICES.map((mc, i) => (\n          <button\n            key={mc.text}\n            type=\"button\"\n            role=\"tab\"\n            aria-selected={choice === i}\n            onClick={() => setChoice(i)}\n            className={cn(\n              \"border-2 border-brutal-ink px-4 py-2 text-sm font-black\",\n              choice === i ? \"bg-brutal-ink text-brutal-paper\" : \"bg-white\",\n            )}\n          >\n            Choice {i === 0 ? \"A\" : \"B\"}\n          </button>\n        ))}\n      </div>\n      <AnswerChoice>{c.text}</AnswerChoice>\n      <p className=\"font-semibold\">Click each piece to find it in the stimulus.</p>\n      <div className=\"space-y-3\">\n        {c.pieces.map((p) => {\n          const key = `${choice}-${p.piece}`;\n          const open = shown.has(key);\n          return (\n            <button\n              key={key}\n              type=\"button\"\n              aria-expanded={open}\n              onClick={() => setShown((prev) => new Set(prev).add(key))}\n              className={cn(\n                \"w-full text-left border-2 border-brutal-ink p-4 transition-colors\",\n                !open && \"bg-white hover:bg-brutal-yellow/40\",\n                open && p.quote && \"bg-brutal-green/30\",\n                open && !p.quote && \"bg-brutal-red/15\",\n              )}\n            >\n              <span className=\"block font-black\">\"{p.piece}\"</span>\n              {open ? (\n                <span className=\"block mt-1 text-base\">\n                  {p.quote ? (\n                    <>\n                      Found: <span className=\"bg-brutal-yellow px-1\">\"{p.quote}\"</span>. {p.note}\n                    </>\n                  ) : (\n                    <>\n                      <span className=\"font-black\">Not found.</span> {p.note}\n                    </>\n                  )}\n                </span>\n              ) : (\n                <span className=\"block mt-1 text-base text-brutal-ink-soft\">Click to match</span>\n              )}\n            </button>\n          );\n        })}\n      </div>\n      {c.pieces.every((p) => shown.has(`${choice}-${p.piece}`)) && (\n        <div\n          className={cn(\n            \"mt-4 border-4 border-brutal-ink p-4 text-lg font-black\",\n            c.pieces.every((p) => p.quote) ? \"bg-brutal-green\" : \"bg-brutal-red text-brutal-paper\",\n          )}\n        >\n          {c.pieces.every((p) => p.quote)\n            ? \"Every piece matches. Choice A stays in.\"\n            : \"One word has nothing to match. Choice B is out, even though the rest of it is fine.\"}\n        </div>\n      )}\n      <p className=\"mt-6\">\n        Do this for every choice you're seriously considering, and always for the last two you're stuck between.\n      </p>\n    </div>\n  );\n}\n\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------- Module 5\n\nconst METHOD_FAMILIES: { family: string; methods: string[] }[] = [\n  {\n    family: \"Arguing about causes\",\n    methods: [\n      \"Offering an alternative explanation\",\n      \"Proving one cause by ruling out the others\",\n      \"Showing the opponent's evidence fits another explanation too\",\n      \"Inferring a cause from a correlation\",\n    ],\n  },\n  {\n    family: \"Leaning on an outside source\",\n    methods: [\"Appealing to a definition\", \"Appealing to an authority\"],\n  },\n  {\n    family: \"Going after the other side\",\n    methods: [\n      \"Pointing out undesirable consequences of a proposal\",\n      \"Pointing out information the opponent overlooked\",\n      \"Attacking a premise of the opponent's argument\",\n      \"Exposing an erroneous assumption\",\n      \"Showing the conclusion leads to an absurd result\",\n      \"Using a counterexample against a general claim\",\n      \"Criticizing an inappropriate comparison\",\n    ],\n  },\n  {\n    family: \"Moving between rules and cases\",\n    methods: [\n      \"Deriving a general principle from specific cases\",\n      \"Applying a generalization to a specific case\",\n      \"Inferring that two groups share a trait because they share others\",\n      \"Using an analogy\",\n    ],\n  },\n];\n\nexport function MethodNote() {\n  return (\n    <div>\n      <Panel>\n        A Method question asks what the author does. The right answer describes the author's move, not the topic.\n      </Panel>\n      <p>\n        Break each choice down exactly as in Module 3. Then ask one question: <strong>did the author do this?</strong>{\" \"}\n        Not \"is this true about the topic.\" Did the author make this move, in this argument?\n      </p>\n\n      <h3>The seventeen moves</h3>\n      <p>Almost every method answer describes one of these. They fall into four families.</p>\n      <div className=\"grid md:grid-cols-2 gap-4 my-6\">\n        {METHOD_FAMILIES.map((f) => (\n          <div key={f.family} className=\"bg-white border-2 border-brutal-ink p-4 brutal-shadow-sm\">\n            <p className=\"m-0 mb-2 font-black\">{f.family}</p>\n            <ul className=\"m-0\">\n              {f.methods.map((m) => (\n                <li key={m}>{m}</li>\n              ))}\n            </ul>\n          </div>\n        ))}\n      </div>\n      <p>Each one has a worked example in the Methods Library in the sidebar.</p>\n\n      <h3>What a method answer is not</h3>\n      <div className=\"space-y-3 my-6\">\n        <RightWrongCard ok={false} title=\"A description of the topic.\">\n          \"The argument discusses school funding\" names a subject, not a move.\n        </RightWrongCard>\n        <RightWrongCard ok={false} title=\"A move the author could have made.\">\n          If the author never compares two things, \"uses an analogy\" is wrong, however reasonable it sounds.\n        </RightWrongCard>\n        <RightWrongCard ok title=\"The move the author actually made.\">\n          You should be able to point to the sentences where it happens.\n        </RightWrongCard>\n      </div>\n    </div>\n  );\n}\n\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------- Module 6\n\nconst TRAPS: { name: string; what: string; example: string }[] = [\n  {\n    name: \"Wrong role\",\n    what: \"Gets the job of the statement wrong.\",\n    example: \"Calls a premise the main conclusion, or calls the author's own support a concession.\",\n  },\n  {\n    name: \"Wrong method\",\n    what: \"Describes a move the author never makes.\",\n    example: \"Says \\\"offers an analogy\\\" when nothing is compared.\",\n  },\n  {\n    name: \"Structural mismatch\",\n    what: \"Names a logical structure the argument doesn't have.\",\n    example:\n      \"Says \\\"confuses necessary and sufficient\\\" when there is no requirement in the argument, or \\\"mistakes time order for cause\\\" when nothing depends on time.\",\n  },\n  {\n    name: \"Attractive language\",\n    what: \"Close to right, but one word is too strong or out of scope.\",\n    example: \"\\\"Proves\\\" for \\\"suggests.\\\" \\\"Always\\\" for \\\"often.\\\" \\\"False\\\" for \\\"not shown.\\\"\",\n  },\n];\n\nexport function TrapsNote() {\n  return (\n    <div>\n      <Panel>\n        Most wrong answers to Role, Method and Flaw questions fall into four traps. Name the trap and the answer is\n        easy to throw out.\n      </Panel>\n      <div className=\"space-y-4 my-6\">\n        {TRAPS.map((t, i) => (\n          <div key={t.name} className=\"bg-white border-2 border-brutal-ink p-4 brutal-shadow-sm\">\n            <p className=\"m-0 font-black\">\n              {i + 1}. {t.name}\n            </p>\n            <p className=\"m-0 mt-1 font-semibold\">{t.what}</p>\n            <p className=\"m-0 mt-2 text-brutal-ink-soft\">{t.example}</p>\n          </div>\n        ))}\n      </div>\n\n      <h3>The one-word trap</h3>\n      <p>\n        This is attractive language at its sneakiest. The answer describes the argument almost perfectly, except\n        for one word. It often sits right next to the correct answer, which tends to be worded more cautiously.\n      </p>\n      <div className=\"space-y-3 my-6\">\n        <RightWrongCard ok={false} title=\"It shows that the officials' explanation is false.\">\n          The author only offered another possible cause. \"False\" is one word too strong.\n        </RightWrongCard>\n        <RightWrongCard ok title=\"It shows that the officials' explanation may not be the only one.\">\n          Cautious, and every piece matches.\n        </RightWrongCard>\n      </div>\n      <p>\n        When two answers look nearly the same, read them word by word. Find the word that's different and ask\n        whether the stimulus earns it.\n      </p>\n    </div>\n  );\n}\n\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------- Module 7\n\nexport function PutTogetherNote() {\n  return (\n    <div>\n      <Panel>Every abstract answer choice gets the same routine. No exceptions for the one you like.</Panel>\n      <p>\n        <strong>1. Break it down.</strong> Keywords, strict meanings, one plain sentence.\n      </p>\n      <p>\n        <strong>2. List what the stimulus must contain.</strong> One item per piece.\n      </p>\n      <p>\n        <strong>3. Point to each piece in the stimulus.</strong> Nouns are there, verbs happen, qualifiers have the\n        right strength.\n      </p>\n      <p>\n        <strong>4. Check the strong words.</strong> One word too strong sinks the choice.\n      </p>\n      <p>\n        The quiz gives you ten answer choices you haven't decoded yet. The drill gives you four full Flaw\n        questions. Run the routine on every choice, not just the first one that sounds right.\n      </p>\n    </div>\n  );\n}\n\nexport const NOTE_COMPONENTS: Record<string, () => JSX.Element> = {\n  why_hard: WhyHardNote,\n  words_to_know: WordsNote,\n  deabstractifying_method: BreakItDownNote,\n  art_of_matching: MatchingNote,\n  master_keys: MethodNote,\n  five_bucket_indicators: TrapsNote,\n  abstract_15: PutTogetherNote,\n};\n"}

The written example in decode\n// items is a self-check, never auto-graded.\n\n// Seeded shuffle so option order is stable for an item but not \"correct first\".\nfunction seededOrder(length: number, seed: string): number[] {\n  let h = 0;\n  for (let i = 0; i < seed.length; i++) h = (h * 31 + seed.charCodeAt(i)) >>> 0;\n  const order = Array.from({ length }, (_, i) => i);\n  for (let i = order.length - 1; i > 0; i--) {\n    h = (h * 1103515245 + 12345) >>> 0;\n    const j = h % (i + 1);\n    [order[i], order[j]] = [order[j], order[i]];\n  }\n  return order;\n}\n\nfunction Feedback({ ok, children }: { ok: boolean; children: React.ReactNode }) {\n  return (\n    <div\n      role=\"status\"\n      className={cn(\n        \"mt-3 border-2 border-brutal-ink p-3 flex gap-3 items-start\",\n        ok ? \"bg-brutal-green/40\" : \"bg-brutal-red/15\",\n      )}\n    >\n      <span\n        className={cn(\n          \"shrink-0 inline-flex items-center gap-1 border-2 border-brutal-ink px-2 py-0.5 text-[11px] font-black uppercase tracking-wider\",\n          ok ? \"bg-brutal-green\" : \"bg-brutal-red text-brutal-paper\",\n        )}\n      >\n        {ok ? <Check className=\"w-3.5 h-3.5\" aria-hidden /> : <X className=\"w-3.5 h-3.5\" aria-hidden />}\n        {ok ? \"Correct\" : \"Not quite\"}\n      </span>\n      <span className=\"font-medium leading-relaxed\">{children}</span>\n    </div>\n  );\n}\n\nfunction StepLabel({ n, children }: { n: number; children: React.ReactNode }) {\n  return (\n    <p className=\"font-black mb-3 mt-6 first:mt-0\">\n      <span className=\"inline-block bg-brutal-ink text-brutal-paper px-2 py-0.5 mr-2 text-xs uppercase tracking-widest\">\n        Step {n}\n      </span>\n      {children}\n    </p>\n  );\n}\n\nfunction OptionButton({\n  children,\n  state,\n  onClick,\n  disabled,\n}: {\n  children: React.ReactNode;\n  state: \"idle\" | \"right\" | \"wrong\";\n  onClick: () => void;\n  disabled?: boolean;\n}) {\n  return (\n    <button\n      type=\"button\"\n      onClick={onClick}\n      disabled={disabled}\n      className={cn(\n        \"w-full text-left border-2 border-brutal-ink p-3 md:p-4 font-medium leading-relaxed transition-colors\",\n        state === \"idle\" && \"bg-white hover:bg-brutal-yellow/40 disabled:hover:bg-white\",\n        state === \"right\" && \"bg-brutal-green\",\n        state === \"wrong\" && \"bg-brutal-red/20 line-through decoration-2\",\n      )}\n    >\n      {children}\n    </button>\n  );\n}\n\n// Multiple-choice step with retry. Calls onSolved(firstTry) once the right\n// option is picked.\nfunction ChoiceStep({\n  seed,\n  choices,\n  onSolved,\n}: {\n  seed: string;\n  choices: Choice[];\n  onSolved: (firstTry: boolean) => void;\n}) {\n  const order = useMemo(() => seededOrder(choices.length, seed), [choices.length, seed]);\n  const [wrongPicks, setWrongPicks] = useState<number[]>([]);\n  const [solved, setSolved] = useState(false);\n  const [last, setLast] = useState<number | null>(null);\n\n  const pick = (i: number) => {\n    if (solved) return;\n    setLast(i);\n    if (choices[i].correct) {\n      setSolved(true);\n      onSolved(wrongPicks.length === 0);\n    } else if (!wrongPicks.includes(i)) {\n      setWrongPicks((w) => [...w, i]);\n    }\n  };\n\n  return (\n    <div>\n      <div className=\"space-y-2\">\n        {order.map((i) => (\n          <OptionButton\n            key={i}\n            onClick={() => pick(i)}\n            disabled={solved || wrongPicks.includes(i)}\n            state={solved && choices[i].correct ? \"right\" : wrongPicks.includes(i) ? \"wrong\" : \"idle\"}\n          >\n            {choices[i].text}\n          </OptionButton>\n        ))}\n      </div>\n      {last !== null && <Feedback ok={choices[last].correct}>{choices[last].why}</Feedback>}\n    </div>\n  );\n}\n\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------- map\n\nfunction MapRunner({ item, onComplete }: { item: MapItem; onComplete: (correct: boolean) => void }) {\n  const [directionWrong, setDirectionWrong] = useState<number[]>([]);\n  const [directionDone, setDirectionDone] = useState(false);\n  const [lastPiece, setLastPiece] = useState<number | null>(null);\n  const [letterWrong, setLetterWrong] = useState<string[]>([]);\n  const [letterDone, setLetterDone] = useState(false);\n  const [lastLetter, setLastLetter] = useState<string | null>(null);\n\n  const isLetter = (p: string) => /^[XYZ],?$/.test(p.trim());\n\n  const pickPiece = (i: number) => {\n    if (directionDone) return;\n    setLastPiece(i);\n    if (i === item.directionIndex) setDirectionDone(true);\n    else if (!directionWrong.includes(i)) setDirectionWrong((w) => [...w, i]);\n  };\n\n  const pickLetter = (l: \"X\" | \"Y\") => {\n    if (letterDone) return;\n    setLastLetter(l);\n    if (l === item.conclusion) {\n      setLetterDone(true);\n      onComplete(directionWrong.length === 0 && letterWrong.length === 0);\n    } else if (!letterWrong.includes(l)) {\n      setLetterWrong((w) => [...w, l]);\n    }\n  };\n\n  return (\n    <div>\n      <StepLabel n={1}>Tap the direction words.</StepLabel>\n      <div className=\"flex flex-wrap gap-2 items-center bg-white border-2 border-brutal-ink p-4\">\n        {item.pieces.map((p, i) => {\n          const right = directionDone && i === item.directionIndex;\n          const wrong = directionWrong.includes(i);\n          return (\n            <button\n              key={i}\n              type=\"button\"\n              onClick={() => pickPiece(i)}\n              disabled={directionDone || wrong}\n              className={cn(\n                \"border-2 px-3 py-2 text-lg font-bold transition-colors\",\n                isLetter(p) ? \"border-brutal-ink bg-brutal-cyan/40 min-w-[48px]\" : \"border-brutal-ink bg-white\",\n                !directionDone && !wrong && \"hover:bg-brutal-yellow/50\",\n                right && \"bg-brutal-green\",\n                wrong && \"bg-brutal-red/20 line-through\",\n              )}\n            >\n              {p}\n            </button>\n          );\n        })}\n      </div>\n      {lastPiece !== null &&\n        (lastPiece === item.directionIndex ? (\n          <Feedback ok>{item.directionWhy}</Feedback>\n        ) : (\n          <Feedback ok={false}>\n            {isLetter(item.pieces[lastPiece])\n              ? \"X and Y are placeholders. Look for the words that link them.\"\n              : \"That word names what the argument does. Look for the words that say which side supports which.\"}\n          </Feedback>\n        ))}\n\n      {directionDone && (\n        <>\n          <StepLabel n={2}>Which letter is the conclusion?</StepLabel>\n          <div className=\"flex gap-3\">\n            {([\"X\", \"Y\"] as const).map((l) => (\n              <button\n                key={l}\n                type=\"button\"\n                onClick={() => pickLetter(l)}\n                disabled={letterDone || letterWrong.includes(l)}\n                className={cn(\n                  \"border-4 border-brutal-ink w-20 h-16 text-2xl font-black transition-colors\",\n                  letterDone && l === item.conclusion && \"bg-brutal-green\",\n                  letterWrong.includes(l) && \"bg-brutal-red/20 line-through\",\n                  !letterDone && !letterWrong.includes(l) && \"bg-white hover:bg-brutal-yellow/50\",\n                )}\n              >\n                {l}\n              </button>\n            ))}\n          </div>\n          {lastLetter !== null &&\n            (lastLetter === item.conclusion ? (\n              <Feedback ok>{item.conclusionWhy}</Feedback>\n            ) : (\n              <Feedback ok={false}>\n                {lastLetter} sits on the evidence side of \"{item.pieces[item.directionIndex]}.\" Try the other letter.\n              </Feedback>\n            ))}\n        </>\n      )}\n    </div>\n  );\n}\n\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------- decode\n\nfunction DecodeRunner({ item, onComplete }: { item: DecodeItem; (\n            <button\n              type=\"button\"\n              disabled={written.trim().length < 20}\n              onClick={() => setCompared(true)}\n              className=\"brutal-btn bg-brutal-yellow mt-3 disabled:opacity-50\"\n            >\n              Compare with a model\n            </button>\n          ) : (\n            <div className=\"mt-4 grid md:grid-cols-2 gap-4\">\n              <div className=\"bg-brutal-yellow/40 border-2 border-brutal-ink p-4\">\n                <span className=\"block text-[11px] font-black uppercase tracking-[0.18em] mb-2\">A model answer</span>\n                <p className=\"m-0 font-medium\">{item.modelExample}</p>\n              </div>\n              <div className=\"bg-white border-2 border-brutal-ink p-4\">\n                <span className=\"block text-[11px] font-black uppercase tracking-[0.18em] mb-2\">\n                  Self-check: does yours have all of these?\n                </span>\n                <ul className=\"list-disc pl-5 m-0 space-y-1\">\n                  {item.modelCheck.map((c) => (\n                    <li key={c} className=\"font-medium\">\n                      {c}\n                    </li>\n                  ))}\n                </ul>\n              </div>\n            </div>\n          )}\n          {compared && selfCheck === null && (\n            <div className=\"flex flex-wrap gap-3 mt-4\">\n              <button\n                type=\"button\"\n                onClick={() => {\n                  setSelfCheck(true);\n                  onComplete(firstTry.current);\n                }}\n                className=\"brutal-btn bg-brutal-green\"\n              >\n                Yes, mine has all of them\n              </button>\n              <button\n                type=\"button\"\n                onClick={() => setSelfCheck(false)}\n                className=\"brutal-btn bg-white\"\n              >\n                Not yet. Let me revise it.\n              </button>\n            </div>\n          )}\n          {selfCheck === false && (\n            <p className=\"mt-3 font-medium\">\n              Edit your argument above until it has every piece on the list, then confirm.\n              <button\n                type=\"button\"\n                onClick={() => {\n                  setSelfCheck(true);\n                  onComplete(firstTry.current);\n                }}\n                className=\"brutal-btn bg-brutal-green ml-3\"\n              >\n                Done revising\n              </button>\n            </p>\n          )}\n        </>\n      )}\n    </div>\n  );\n}\n\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------- build\n\nfunction BuildRunner({ item, onComplete }: { item: BuildItem; onComplete: (correct: boolean) => void }) {\n  const orders = useMemo(\n    () => item.slots.map((s, i) => seededOrder(s.options.length, `${item.id}-${i}`)),\n    [item],\n  );\n  const [chosen, setChosen] = useState<(number | null)[]>(item.slots.map(() => null));\n  const [checked, setChecked] = useState(false);\n  const [everWrong, setEverWrong] = useState(false);\n  const [done, setDone] = useState(false);\n\n  const allChosen = chosen.every((c) => c !== null);\n  const allRight = chosen.every((c, i) => c === item.slots[i].correct);\n\n  const check = () => {\n    setChecked(true);\n    if (allRight) {\n      setDone(true);\n      onComplete(!everWrong);\n    } else {\n      setEverWrong(true);\n    }\n  };\n\n  return (\n    <div>\n      <ol className=\"list-none p-0 m-0 space-y-2 mb-4\">\n        {item.sentences.map((s, i) => (\n          <li\n            key={i}\n            className={cn(\n              \"border-2 border-brutal-ink p-3 font-medium leading-relaxed\",\n              item.focus === i ? oi : v)));\n                      setChecked(false);\n                    }}\n                    state={\n                      picked === oi\n                        ? checked\n                          ? slotRight\n                            ? \"right\"\n                            : \"wrong\"\n                          : \"idle\"\n                        : \"idle\"\n                    }\n                  >\n                    <span className={cn(picked === oi && !checked && \"font-black\")}>\n                      {picked === oi && !checked ? \"▸ \" : \"\"}\n                      {slot.options[oi]}\n                    </span>\n                  </OptionButton>\n                ))}\n              </div>\n              {checked && picked !== null && (\n                <Feedback ok={slotRight}>\n                  {slotRight ? border-b-2 border-brutal-ink\">&nbsp;</span>\n                ))}\n            </span>\n          ))}\n        </p>\n      </div>\n\n      {!done && (\n        <button\n          type=\"button\"\n          disabled={!allChosen}\n          onClick={check}\n          className=\"brutal-btn bg-brutal-yellow mt-4 disabled:opacity-50\"\n        >\n          {checked && !allRight ? \"Check again\" : onDone: () => void }) {\n  const { markDrillComplete, markModuleDrillComplete, addXp } = useApp();\n  const [index, setIndex] = useState(0);\n  const [results, setResults] = useState<Record<string, BreakdownItem = BREAKDOWN_ITEMS[index];\n  const part = BREAKDOWN_PARTS.find((p) => p.kind === item.kind)!;\n  const firstOfPart = index === 0 || BREAKDOWN_ITEMS[index - 1].kind !== item.kind;\n  const answered = results[item.id] !== undefined;\n  const answeredCount = Object.keys(results).length;\n  const pct = Math.round((answeredCount / BREAKDOWN_ITEMS.length) * 100);\n\n  const complete = (correct: boolean) => {\n    if (answered) return;\n    setResults((r) => ({ ...r, [item.id]: correct }));\n    if (correct) addXp(5);\n    void markDrillComplete(item.id, \"translation\", correct, setFinished(true);\n    } else {\n      setIndex((i) => i + 1);\n    }\n  };\n\n  if (finished) {\n    const firstTry = Object.values(results).filter(Boolean).length;\n    return (\n      <div font-medium mb-6\">\n          {firstTry} of {BREAKDOWN_ITEMS.length} right on the first try.\n        </p>\n        <button type=\"button\" onClick={onDone} className=\"brutal-btn bg-brutal-green inline-flex items-center gap-2\">\n          Finish module <ArrowRight className=\"w-4 h-4\" />\n        </button>\n      </div>\n    );\n  }\n\n  return (\n    <div>\n      <div className=\"mb-4\">\n        <div className=\"flex items-center justify-between mb-1.5\">\n          <p className=\"text-[11px] font-black uppercase tracking-widest text-brutal-ink-soft\">\n            Item {index + 1} of className=\"h-2.5 bg-brutal-ink-trace border-2 border-brutal-ink\"\n          role=\"progressbar\"\n          aria-valuenow={pct}\n          aria-valuemin={0}\n          aria-valuemax={100}\n          aria-label=\"Drill progress\"\n        >\n          <div className=\"h-full bg-brutal-ink transition-[width] duration-300\" style={{ width: `${pct}%` }} />\n        </div>\n      </div>\n\n      <div className=\"text-xl font-black m-0\">{part.title}</h3>\n            <p className=\"m-0 mt-1 font-medium text-brutal-ink-soft\">{part.instruction}</p>\n          </div>\n        )}\n        {item.kind === \"map\" && <MapRunner key={item.id} item={item} onComplete={complete} />}\n        {item.kind === \"decode\" && <DecodeRunner key={item.id} item={item} onComplete={complete} />}\n        {item.kind === \"build\" && <BuildRunner key={item.id} item={item} onComplete={complete} />}\n\n        {answered && (\n          <div className=\"mt-6 pt-4 border-t-2 border-brutal-ink flex
